An idea struck Peter. “I wonder what Joanna would say to that!” He rushed in the house, seized a piece of paper and a pen and told her about it.

===Chinese===
他一路想着心事，朝隆巴德街走去。「那姑娘可真懂你的心。能碰上这样一副性子的人，真好。当然，我还是宁愿当外科医生。可我受够了这没完没了的苦熬。这近七年，我活得跟个奴隶没两样，外带穷得叮当响。老天爷，一想到本可以从你们身上挣出的那些钱！」他望着自己那双漂亮修长的手，指端方方整整，结实有力。

「天生的外科医生的手。」达文南特医生曾这样对他的助手说。

彼得灵光一闪。「不知道乔安娜听了这个会怎么说！」他冲进屋子，抓过一张纸一支笔，把这话写信告诉了她。

===Original===
> “Of course, Jan, I don’t expect you to marry me if I can’t take care of you. You wouldn’t anyway, you’re not like Sylvia. That’s not a slam, dearest, that’s just a plain statement of facts. But I’m making a lot of money right now﻿—guess how?﻿—with my music, playing for ‘grand white folks’ at all the swell society functions. Of course it takes me out of my classes sometimes, but I don’t care, I’m fed up with all that. I’ve got such a Negro-loving bunch of professors, except my surgical men.

> “What say, Joanna, if I quit this, and we get married and I go about the country with you as your accompanist? That ought to suit you, for I don’t suppose you ever dream of settling down.

> “Did I tell you I met Maggie Ellersley? I see her very often. The fellow I play with lives in the same house she does. In fact, Maggie introduced me to him. She’s been no end kind to me. You’ll be interested to know she’s getting a divorce from that beast she married. See what Philip has to say when you tell him.

> “Mind you write me right away what you think about this.”

===Chinese===
> 「当然啦，小乔，我要是养不起你，就不指望你嫁给我。反正你也不会嫁——你不像西尔维娅。这不是损人，最亲爱的，这只是照实陈述事实。可我这阵子正挣着大钱呢——猜猜怎么挣的？——靠我的音乐，在所有阔气的社交盛会上给『高等白人老爷』演奏。这当然有时会误了我的课，可我不在乎，那一套我受够了。我那些教授一个个『热爱黑人』热爱得不得了，教外科的那几位除外。

> 「乔安娜，你说怎么样：要是我撂下这一摊，咱们结了婚，我当你的伴奏，陪你走遍全国？这该合你的心意，因为我想你压根儿没梦见过安顿下来。

> 「我有没有告诉过你，我遇见玛吉·埃勒斯利了？我常常见她。跟我同台演奏的那个小伙子，就住在她那栋楼里。其实是玛吉把我引见给他的。她待我好得没法说。你准有兴趣知道：她正跟嫁的那个畜生闹离婚。等你告诉菲利普，看他有什么说的。

> 「记着，马上写信告诉我，你对此到底怎么想。」

===Original===
The answer came posthaste.

> “What I think about this,” [wrote Joanna, infuriated] “is that I don’t want and won’t have a husband who is just an ordinary strumming accompanist, playing one, two, three, one, two, three. Sometimes, Peter, I think you must be crazy.”

A number of irritable and irritating notes followed on both sides until a couple of weeks before Christmas, when both sank into a mutinous silence.

===Chinese===
回信火速就到。

> 「我对此怎么想，」〔乔安娜怒不可遏地写道〕「就是：我不想要、也决不要一个光会平平常常拨弄伴奏的丈夫，一、二、三，一、二、三，拨弄个没完。彼得，有时候我想，你准是疯了。」

此后双方你来我往，好几封怄气又气人的信，直到圣诞节前两周，两人一齐沉入一场赌着气的沉默。

===Original===
What Peter did not understand and what Joanna never knew he needed explained to him was that she wanted Peter to be somebody for his own sake. She was really paying him a sincere compliment when she told him that she did not want an accompanist for a husband. Like many a woman of strong and purposeful character, she hated a weak man. It followed then that the man who won Joanna must be even stronger, more determined than she.

She did not know much about marriage. She had not only the usual virginal ignorance of many American girls, she had also a remarkable lack of curiosity on the matter. But she knew vaguely that the man was supposed to be the head. How could she, Joanna Marshall, ever surrender to a man who was less than she in any respect? Her dominating nature craved one still more dominant. But neither Peter nor she knew this, she least of all. Youth, egotistic though it be, is notably free from this kind of introspection.

Since American customs of courtship give the girl largely the upper hand, Joanna was instinctively, if unanalytically, using Peter’s love for her, and her own desirability, as a whip to goad him on. It was hard for her, too, much harder than Peter knew, or than she realized. For she was beginning at last to feel the tug of passion at her heartstrings. It would never have occurred to her to marry Peter before he was in their common estimation “on his feet,” she would never have asked it of him, she did not expect him to ask it of her. But unconsciously she was yearning for the day when the two might join hands and enter the portals which lead to the house of life.

===Chinese===
彼得不明白、而乔安娜又从不知道他正需要有人给他点破的是：她要彼得为他自己而成个人物。她告诉他自己不要一个当伴奏的丈夫，其实是在真心实意地夸他。同许多性子刚强、目标笃定的女子一样，她厌恶软弱的男人。由此便推出一条：凡要赢得乔安娜的男人，必须比她更强、更有决断。

论婚姻，她懂得不多。她不但有着许多美国姑娘常有的那种闺中无知，而且在这件事上还出奇地缺乏好奇心。但她模模糊糊地知道，男人理应是一家之主。她，乔安娜·马歇尔，怎么可能屈从一个无论哪方面都不如自己的男人？她那称霸的天性，渴求的是一个更加霸气的人。可这一点彼得不知道，她也不知道——她最不知道。青春，尽管自我中心，偏偏绝少做这类自我省察。

既然美国的求偶风俗把主动权大半交在姑娘手里，乔安娜便凭着本能——虽未经细想——拿彼得对她的爱、拿她自身的可爱，当作一根鞭子，策他向前。这对她也是煎熬，比彼得所知道的、也比她自己意识到的难熬得多。因为她终于开始觉出激情在心弦上的那份牵扯。若不是照两人共同的标准他已经「站稳脚跟」，她绝不会起嫁他的念头；她决不会向他提，也不指望他向她提。但她无意识地渴盼着那一天：两人携起手来，跨进那通往人生之殿的门廊。

===Original===
Very often she found herself vaguely glad that she had her work. Without it, what would she have done? What *did* girls do while they waited for their young men? Heavens, how awful to be sitting around listlessly from day to day, waiting, waiting! Anything was better than that, even pounding a typewriter in a box of an office. It was this lack of interest and purpose on the part of girls which brought about so many hasty marriages which terminated in﻿—no, not poverty﻿—mediocrity. Joanna hated the word; with her visual mind she saw it embodied in broken chairs, cold gravy, dingy linen, sticky children. She would never mind poverty half so much; she would contrive somehow to climb out of that. But ordinary tame mediocrity!

Besides, colored people had had enough of that. Not for Joanna!

It must not be thought that at this time she had any intention of relinquishing her work after marriage. But it was for that reason that she wanted Peter to come out of the herd. She saw the two of them together, gracious, shining, perfect! She heard whispers:

“That’s Peter Bye, the distinguished surgeon! His wife is unusual, too, she was Joanna Marshall. You must have heard of her. Why, she sings all over the country!”

And here was Peter offering her the vision of herself, standing glorious, resplendent in her stage clothes, while he trailed across to the piano, her music portfolio under his arm:

===Chinese===
她常常隐隐觉得庆幸，亏得自己有份事业。没有它，她能干什么呢？姑娘们等着心上人的时候，都干些什么呢？天哪，一天天没精打采地闲坐着，等啊，等啊，多可怕！什么都比那个强，哪怕在鸽子笼似的办公室里敲打字机。正是姑娘们的这种没兴趣、没目标，招来了那么多仓促的婚姻，而其下场是——不，不是贫穷——是平庸。乔安娜恨这个词；凭她那双善于具象的眼睛，她看见它化身为一把把破椅子、凉透的肉汁、发乌的桌布、黏糊糊的孩子。贫穷她倒一点儿不放在心上，总归有法子从里头爬出来。可这种规规矩矩、温温吞吞的平庸！

何况，有色人种受够这个了。乔安娜可不要！

别以为她这时存着婚后放弃事业的念头。但恰恰是为了这个，她才要彼得从人堆里拔出来。她看见两人并肩而立，风致楚楚，光彩照人，完美无缺！她听见窃窃私语：

「那位是彼得·拜，杰出的外科医生！他太太也不同凡响，她原先就是乔安娜·马歇尔。你一定听说过她。可不是嘛，她全国各地演唱呢！」

而眼下彼得献给她的，却是她自己这样一幅图景：站在台上，一身戏装，辉煌夺目，他却胳臂底下夹着她的乐谱夹，跟在后头走向钢琴：

===Original===
“That’s Peter Bye!”

“Peter Bye? Who’s he?”

“The husband of Joanna Marshall, the artist.”

She would never endure it.

===Chinese===
「那是彼得·拜！」

「彼得·拜？他是谁？」

「艺术家乔安娜·马歇尔的丈夫。」

这个她决计忍受不了。

===Original===
“And I don’t thank Maggie Ellersley the least bit for introducing him to this music man, whoever he is,” she told herself after she had read the letter. “Tell Philip she’s getting a divorce indeed! How much would any decent man be interested in her after that?”

Poor inexperienced Joanna!

Peter’s vagaries were not her only worries. She was undergoing just now what she would have termed a really serious disappointment. Her dancing, on which she had spent so many years, so much of her father’s and her own money, on which she had built so many high hopes, was destined, it seemed, to avail her nothing.

She had been so sure. Her art was so perfect, so complete that even Bertully, cynic though he was, believed that in her case the American stage must let down the bars.

===Chinese===
「还有，那个搞音乐的是谁我不知道，可玛吉·埃勒斯利把彼得引见给他，我半点也不领情。」读完信她对自己说。「闹离婚，还要告诉菲利普！往后还有几分指望，哪个正派男人会看得上她？」

可怜的、毫无阅历的乔安娜！

彼得的反复无常还不是她唯一的心事。她眼下正经历着一桩她自己会称之为真正严重的失意。她那门舞蹈——多少年的工夫、她父亲的和她自己的那么多钱都花在上面，那么多高远的希望都筑在上面——看样子，注定要一场空。

她原是那样有把握。她的艺术那样完美、那样圆熟，连贝图利——虽是个惯于讥诮的人——也相信，单为她一人，美国的舞台也必定得撤了那道栅栏。

===Original===
“They have but to see you, Mademoiselle, to *réaliser* zat you are somebody, zat you have ze great gift. And when they see you to danse, v’la!” He snapped his thin fingers. Joanna, he told his assistant, Madame Céleste, was the best pupil he’d ever had.

“You look at her and she is ze child, so grave, so *sage*. In another moment she is like a wild creature, a Bacchante. Onless zey are all fools, these *Américains*, they take her up, *hein* Céleste?”

Madame Céleste nodded a dark, assenting head.

Bertully himself accompanied her. There were three or four managers for whom he had done favors.

===Chinese===
「他们只消见您一眼，小姐，便会明白您是个人物，明白您身怀大才。等他们见您一跳舞，瞧吧！」他瘦削的手指打了个响指。他告诉助手塞莱斯特夫人（Madame Céleste），乔安娜是他带过的最好的学生。

「你看她，这时是个孩子，那么沉静，那么乖巧；一转眼，她又像一头野性难驯的生灵，一位酒神女祭司（Bacchante）。这些美国人除非全是傻瓜，否则准会把她捧起来，嗯，塞莱斯特？」

塞莱斯特夫人那颗深色的头赞同地点了点。

贝图利亲自陪她前往。有三四位经理，都欠过他的人情。

===Original===
They went first to a Mr. Abrams, who received Joanna kindly. “I’m sure of your ability, my dear girl, and you ought to go. You’re young. I can see you could be made into a beauty. With Bertully recommending you as he does, you must be a wizard. But the white American public ain’t ready for you yet, they won’t have you.”

He looked at her reflectively a few seconds.

“I know the day is coming, but not for some time yet. That don’t console you much, does it? I’ve got an idea of my own, if I think I can put it over, I’ll send for you.”

“Courage,” said Bertully, helping her into the taxi, “there are some others.”

===Chinese===
他们头一个去见艾布拉姆斯先生（Abrams），他对乔安娜接待得很客气。「你的本事我信得过，亲爱的姑娘，你也该往这条路上走。你年轻。我看得出，你可以造就成一个美人。贝图利这样力荐你，你必是个奇才。可美国的白人观众还没预备好接纳你，他们不会要你。」

他若有所思地端详了她几秒钟。

「我知道这一天会来，只是还早。这话安慰不了你多少，对不对？我自己有个主意，要是觉得办得成，我会叫人来请你。」

「鼓起勇气来，」贝图利一面扶她上出租汽车一面说，「还有几家呢。」

===Original===
The next manager, David Kohler, was explicit and to the point. “Couldn’t make any money out of you. America doesn’t want to see a colored dancer in the role of a première danseuse. How’s that accent, Bertully? She wants you to be absurd, grotesque. Of course,” tentatively, “you couldn’t consider being corked up﻿—you’re brown but you’re too light as you are﻿—and doing a breakdown?”

“No,” said Joanna shortly, “I couldn’t. Shall we go, Monsieur?”

By the time they reached the third manager, Joanna for all her natural assurance had become a little timid. Bertully’s name had gained them almost instant admission to the manager, but it was hard in the short wait to listen to the scarcely veiled comments of the office girls and the other applicants.

===Chinese===
第二位经理大卫·科勒（David Kohler）说得直截了当。「从你身上挣不着钱。美国不想看一个有色人种舞者来演首席女舞星（première danseuse）。那口腔调怎么样，贝图利？人家美国要的是你滑稽、你怪诞。当然，」他试探着说，「你就不能考虑把脸涂黑——你是棕色皮肤，可照现在这样又嫌太浅——跳上一段跺脚快舞？」

「不能，」乔安娜简短地答，「我不干。先生，咱们走吧？」

等到了第三位经理那里，乔安娜纵有天生的自信，也变得有些怯了。贝图利的名头使他们几乎立刻获准进见，可等候的那一小会儿里，听办公室女职员和其他应试者的那些几乎不加遮掩的议论，实在难熬。

===Original===
“Say, what do you suppose she is?”

“Must be a South American.”

“She ain’t, she’s a nigger or I don’t know one.”

“Say, she’s got her nerve comin’ here. Think Snyder’ll give her anything?”

“Will he? Not a chance!”

Her cheeks were so flushed when she went in that she really was beautiful. But Snyder gave her one look, checked himself in the act of raising his hat, swung around to the Frenchman.

===Chinese===
「喂，你猜她是什么人？」

「准是南美人。」

「才不是呢，她就是个黑鬼，不然我就不认得什么叫黑鬼。」

「嘿，她可真敢往这儿闯。你以为斯奈德（Snyder）会给她什么吗？」

「他会吗？门儿都没有！」

她进去的时候两颊烧得通红，人真是美极了。可斯奈德瞥了她一眼，抬手摘帽摘到一半又生生顿住，一转身冲着那法国人。

===Original===
“This your great find, Bertully?”

“*Mais oui*,” the old man began excitedly.

The other calmly lit a big black cigar.

“You needn’t wait, Miss. Like to oblige you, Bertully, but I couldn’t do a thing for you.” He walked across the office, held the door open for them, bent over Bertully’s ear. “You’ll ruin your trade teachin’ niggers, Bertully. Better take my tip.”

They rode down in the elevator in silence. Joanna, scarlet to the ears, saw the conjectures written in the eyes of the other passengers as they observed her and the Frenchman’s elaborate courtesies. She would take up no more of his time, she told him, thanking him for his kindness; she would go home now. He understood and beckoned her a taxi, into which he helped her with another elaborate display of courtesy, much to the interest of several spectators.

===Chinese===
「这就是你发掘出来的大宝贝，贝图利？」

「可不是嘛。」老头儿兴奋起来。

对方不慌不忙，点起一支粗黑的雪茄。

「你不必再等了，小姐。很愿意效劳，贝图利，可我无能为力。」他穿过办公室，替他们拉开门，又俯到贝图利耳边。「教黑鬼学舞，会砸了你这行买卖，贝图利。还是听我一句劝吧。」

两人在电梯里一言不发地下了楼。乔安娜红到了耳根，眼见其他乘客打量她和这位法国人的繁复礼数时，眼睛里写着的种种猜度。她不愿再占用他的时间了，她说，多谢他的好意；她这就回家。他心领神会，招手替她唤来一辆出租汽车，又是一场殷勤周到的搀扶送她上车，惹得几个旁观者好一阵注目。

===Original===
“So silly of me to mind this,” Joanna scolded herself. But she did mind it. How could it be possible that she, Joanna Marshall, was meeting with rebuffs? Not that she was conceited. The point was that she had grown up in her own and Joel’s belief﻿—namely, that honest effort led invariably to success. This was probably the first time in her life that she had been thwarted. She was like a spoiled child, bewildered and indignant at being suddenly brought to book.

The week before Christmas a note came from Peter.

“Of course I’ve been planning as usual to come home, Jan. But we haven’t been hitting it off so well lately. Thought I’d better write and see if you really wanted me to.”

She wrote him. “Of course I want you.” Heavens, what would Christmas be without Peter!

===Chinese===
「我为这个介意，真傻。」乔安娜数落自己。可她确实介意。她，乔安娜·马歇尔，怎么可能碰钉子？倒不是她自负。要害在于：她从小到大，信的一直是她自己和乔尔的那条信念——诚实的努力必定通向成功。这恐怕是她生平头一遭受挫。她像个宠坏了的孩子，突然被拉来问罪，又惶惑，又愤慨。

圣诞节前那个礼拜，彼得来了张便条。

「当然，我照例一直在打算回家过节，小乔。可咱们近来处得不太顺。我想还是先写个信，看看你是不是真要我回来。」

她回信道：「我当然要你。」天哪，没有彼得的圣诞节算什么圣诞节！

===Original===
He told her on what train he was arriving and asked her to meet it. She might have done so, but her day was as usual very full and she had a rehearsal at six﻿—of indefinite length. She would have to cut out something. Too bad it had to be meeting Peter. But he surely would come up to the house at once.

Her accompanist appeared promptly and they put in a hard two hours. Joanna, her ear unconsciously straining for the telephone or the doorbell, was not up to her usual mark. Eight o’clock and Peter not here and his train in at four! Well, he wasn’t coming then. She plunged into hard work. Her father came by the door and watched her, thinking what a picture she made in her pretty dress. She had put on one of her old stage frocks, for she usually did better work if she created for herself, as nearly as possible, the atmosphere of the stage. At nine-thirty the accompanist left.

“We went rather slowly at first, but you came out splendidly at the end, Miss Marshall. You were a little bit tired, perhaps.”

“That must have been it. Thank you and good night, Miss Eggleston.”

===Chinese===
他告诉了她乘哪一班火车到，请她去接。她本可以去，可她那天照例排得满满的，六点钟还有一场排练——时长没个准。总得裁掉一件事。糟糕的是裁掉的偏偏是接彼得。不过他总归会马上就到家来的。

伴奏人员准时到了，两人结结实实练了两个钟头。乔安娜的耳朵却不由自主地支棱着，听电话响，听门铃响，状态便不比往常。八点了，彼得人还没到，他的火车可是四点就进站的！好吧，那就是不来了。她一头扎进苦练里。父亲打门口经过，驻足望着她，心想她穿着那身漂亮衣裳，真像一幅画。她套上了一件自己的旧戏装，因为若能给自己造出尽可能贴近舞台的气氛，她通常还发挥得更好。九点半，伴奏走了。

「开头我们进得慢了些，可末了您发挥得漂亮极了，马歇尔小姐。也许您是有点儿累了。」

「想必就是这么回事。谢谢您，晚安，埃格尔斯顿小姐（Miss Eggleston）。」

===Original===
Still no Peter! “Mean thing, I’ll fix him for that.”

The bell buzzed softly, she could barely hear it. Yes, that was he. She heard her father’s voice, “In the back parlor, Bye.”

He came in, came toward her. “Well, Joanna, here’s the wanderer returned.” He bent to kiss her.

She turned him a cold cheek, which to her surprise he kissed without expostulation.

He crossed the room, sat down and looked at her. “H’m, how stagy we are in that getup!”

===Chinese===
还是没有彼得！「狠心的小东西，看我怎么收拾他。」

门铃轻轻嗡了一声，她几乎没听见。是的，是他。她听见父亲的声音：「在后客厅，拜。」

他走进来，朝她走来。「嗳，乔安娜，浪子回来了。」他俯下身要吻她。

她侧给他一面冷冰冰的脸颊，不料他一声不吭就吻了下去。

他穿过房间，坐下，端详着她。「哼，这身行头，咱们的戏台味儿可真足！」

===Original===
He was different somehow, she thought, vaguely hurt by his remark. One of her reasons for putting on the dress had been so that she might please him. She asked him a question to hide her chagrin.

“Where’ve you been, Peter? I thought your train got in at four?”

“It did, but since you weren’t there to meet me, I supposed you didn’t care whether I came late or early, or not at all. I met Vera Manning in the station and took her to a movie.”

Her spirits went up at that. This was just pique, sheer pique.

“How lovely for Vera! And now I’ve got to send you home almost right away. I’ve had a hard day and I’m dreadfully tired. Tell you what, dear boy, come to luncheon tomorrow. We’ll have it together, just we two.”

===Chinese===
他不知怎么变了，她想，被这话刺得隐隐作痛。她穿这身衣裳，理由之一本就是为了讨他喜欢。她问了句什么，好遮掩心中的懊恼。

「你上哪儿去了，彼得？我以为你的火车四点就到？」

「是四点到的。可你既然没去接我，我想你是压根儿不在乎我来得早晚、还是来不来。我在车站碰上了维拉·曼宁，带她看电影去了。」

她的情绪顿时上来了。这不过是赌气，纯粹的赌气。

「维拉可真有福气！不过现在，我差不多得马上就打发你回家了。我今天忙了一天，累得要命。这么着吧，好孩子，明天来吃午饭。咱们俩一块儿吃，就咱们两个。」

===Original===
She thought after he had gone that he had looked at her critically, impersonally.

“As though he were contrasting me with someone,” she murmured.

The next day confirmed her impression. Joanna asked him to praise the luncheon.

“I fixed it every bit myself.”

“I should think so, so feminine and knickknackish.” His tone said: “I’m used to having my taste consulted.”

===Chinese===
他走后她想起，他方才看她的眼神是挑剔的、不涉私情的。

「就好像他在拿我跟什么人作比较。」她喃喃自语。

第二天印证了她的感觉。乔安娜请他夸夸这顿午饭。

「全是我一个人做的。」

「我看得出来，这么女人气，净是小摆设小零碎。」他那语气分明是说：「我惯了让人家来请教我的品味。」

===Original===
Joanna did not like the remark, but there was nothing really to be said about it. She sprang up lightly, began to clear away.

“Come on, lazy Peter Bye, don’t leave everything for me to do.”

He lounged in his chair. “Oh, come, Joanna, I’m used to being waited on, not doing the waiting.”

She stared at him then. “Well, good heavens! What on earth has been happening to you in Philadelphia?”

He spoke from a contented reminiscence. “When I have dinner at Maggie Neal’s, she’s not everlastingly asking me to do this and do that. ‘Sit still, Peter,’ she says, ‘this isn’t a man’s work.’”

===Chinese===
乔安娜不喜欢这话，可又实在没什么好驳的。她轻快地一跃而起，动手收拾。

「来呀，懒骨头彼得·拜，别什么都撂给我一个人干。」

他瘫在椅子里。「嗳，得了，乔安娜，我惯的是让人伺候，不是伺候人。」

她这才直愣愣瞪住他。「天哪！你在费城到底出了什么事？」

他这话是从一段心满意足的追想里说出来的。「我在玛吉·尼尔（Maggie Neal）家吃饭的时候，她从不没完没了地支使我干这干那。『坐着别动，彼得，』她说，『这不是男人干的活儿。』」

===Original===
“Maggie Neal has her own methods with her men friends. Personally I prefer to have mine wait on me.”

He rose to his feet. “Oh, yes, Queen Joanna must be served.”

They finished and went to the parlor. Joanna sang one or two of her songs to his accompaniment. The incident rankled, though she wouldn’t let herself speak about it.

“But he certainly is changed,” she said to herself in an angry bewilderment.

She had to sing in Orange that night and did not intend to return until the next morning.

===Chinese===
「玛吉·尼尔对付她的男性朋友，自有她的一套办法。就我本人而言，我宁可让我的女朋友伺候我。」

他站起身来。「哦，是了，乔安娜女王是必须有人伺候的。」

两人吃完进了客厅。乔安娜在他的伴奏下唱了一两支自己的歌。那桩事在心头作痛，虽然她不许自己说出口。

「可他真是变了。」她又气又惑地对自己说。

那天晚上她要去奥兰治（Orange）演唱，本打算第二天早晨才回来。

===Original===
“What do we do tomorrow?” Peter asked.

“Remember you said you wanted to hear *Aida*? I phoned them to reserve tickets for us for tomorrow’s matinée. But they have to be called for. Better go down there first thing in the morning, Peter.”

He twisted around on the piano stool. “You’ll be down town tomorrow morning coming from Orange. Why don’t you stop for them?”

She couldn’t believe her ears. “Peter Bye, you *are* spoilt,” she flamed. “You’re﻿—why you’re absolutely disgusting. We’ll never hear *Aida* if you depend on my getting the tickets. As long as he was well and not busy, there’s no man in the world I’d do it for.”

“Married women do it for their husbands.”

“Sylvia doesn’t do it for Brian. He wouldn’t dream of asking her. Besides, that’s different. And, anyway, we’re not married yet. Nor likely to be, if we don’t get along any better than this. Whatever’s come over you, Peter?”

===Chinese===
「明天咱们干什么？」彼得问。

「记得你说过想听《阿依达》（Aida）吧？我打电话叫他们替咱们留了明天日场的票。可票得亲自去取。最好明儿一早头一件事就是过去取，彼得。」

他在琴凳上拧过身子。「你明天早上从奥兰治回来，正好顺路进城。干吗你不顺道把票取了？」

她简直不敢相信自己的耳朵。「彼得·拜，你真是给惯坏了，」她冒起火来。「你——哼，你简直叫人恶心。要是指望我去取票，咱们就永远别想听上《阿依达》。只要他身子好好的、事情不忙，天底下没有哪个男人，是我不肯为他取票的。」

「结了婚的女人倒会替丈夫取。」

「西尔维娅就不替布莱恩取。他做梦也不会开这个口。再说，那是两码事。况且不管怎么说，咱们还没结婚呢。要是咱们相处得不能比眼下更好，怕也结不成。你到底是怎么了，彼得？」

===Original===
He shrugged his shoulders. “I think you make a lot of fuss over nothing, Joanna. But all right, I’ll get you the tickets. See you at one-thirty?”

She sat a long time in her room after he had gone, her hands and eyes busy with her day’s mail, which Sylvia always placed on her writing table. But her mind could not take in the written words, it was too full of something else.

But Peter, Peter of all men to act like this! Both she and Sylvia had always known that Maggie was unexacting. The marvel was, however, that Peter should take so quickly to this kind of treatment. Well, she’d just have to hold him that much closer to the mark. He’d see that there were some girls who knew what was due them.

It was time for her to dress. As she looked into the mirror she voiced her real regret. “Two days of the vacation gone, and we’ve done nothing but quarrel. Today he didn’t even ask me for a kiss. Peter, you wretch. Just wait till you come to your senses!”

===Chinese===
他耸了耸肩。「我看你这是没事闹腾，乔安娜。不过好吧，我去取票。一点半见？」

他走后，她在自己房里坐了许久，手和眼睛忙着对付当天的邮件——那总是西尔维娅搁在她写字台上的。可她的脑子装不进那些字句，它早被别的东西挤满了。

可是彼得，天底下那么多男人，偏偏是彼得做出这种样子！她同西尔维娅一向都知道，玛吉是从不苛求人的。稀奇的是，彼得竟这么快就贪上了这一套款待。好吧，那她就更得攥紧他、盯牢他的准头。他要明白，有些姑娘是懂得自己该受什么分量的对待的。

该更衣了。她望着镜子，说出了心底真正的遗憾。「假期过去了两天，咱们净顾着吵架了。今天他连一个吻都没向我讨。彼得，你这个没良心的。只等你回过神来，有你好瞧的！」

===Original===
They were a little stiff next day on the way to the matinée, talking politely and impersonally about the weather in Philadelphia and New York, Joanna’s concert, and Sylvia’s children. Walking up Broadway, however, they thawed a little. Joanna as usual was looking trim. She wore that winter an extremely trig tobacco-brown suit, with a fur turban and a narrow neckpiece of raccoon, the light part setting off the bronze distinction of her face. But Peter was superlative. His financial success with Tom Mason had made it possible for him to indulge in a new outfit which emphasized the distinction of his carriage, set off his handsome face. Several people looked at him on the crowded street. Joanna herself stole several glances sidewise.

He caught her at it. “Joanna Marshall, if you look at me again like that, just once more, mind you, I’ll snatch you up in my arms this minute and kiss you.”

“You wouldn’t dare.”

“I dare you to try it. I’d do it no matter how much you kicked and struggled. Wouldn’t the people stare?”

===Chinese===
第二天在去日场的路上，两人有点儿僵，客客气气、不涉私情地谈着费城和纽约的天气、乔安娜的音乐会、西尔维娅的孩子们。可一走上百老汇，两人便回暖了些。乔安娜照例穿得干净利落。那年冬天，她穿一身极其俏挺的烟草棕套装，戴一顶皮帽，围一条窄窄的浣熊皮领，那浅色的一截正衬出她脸庞青铜色的气韵。可是彼得更胜一筹。同汤姆·梅森搭档挣下的进项，让他得以添置了一身新行头，把这副气派的身架、这张英俊的脸又提亮了一层。拥挤的街上，好几个人朝他望。乔安娜自己也偷偷侧目了好几回。

他逮了个正着。「乔安娜·马歇尔，你要是再那样看我一眼——就一眼，记住——我这会儿就一把把你搂进怀里吻你。」

「你不敢。」

「你敢就试试看。任你蹬着腿挣扎，我照样干。看人们不瞪大了眼？」

===Original===
Joanna giggled. “Can’t you see the headlines in the papers tomorrow? ‘Burly Negro Attacks Strapping Negress on Broadway!’”

“Yes, and the small type underneath, ‘An interested crowd gathered about a pair of dusky combatants yesterday. A Negro and Negress﻿—’”

Joanna interrupted: “Both of them spelt with a small *n*, remember! Here we are at the Opera.”

He caught her hand. “Just because you jockeyed me out of that kiss that time, clever Joanna, doesn’t mean that I’m going to do without it forever.”

In her heart she loved him. “Oh, Peter, be like this always,” she prayed.

===Chinese===
乔安娜咯咯直笑。「你瞧不见明天报纸上的大标题吗？『彪悍黑人在百老汇袭击壮硕黑人女子！』」

「是啊，还有底下的小字：『昨日，围观人群聚拢在一对黝黑的斗士身旁。一黑人男子与一黑人女子——』」

乔安娜打断他：「记住，这两个词打头的 *n* 都是小写的！咱们到歌剧院了。」

他一把抓住她的手。「聪明的乔安娜，那回你耍手段赖掉了那个吻，可不等于我从此就永远不要了。」

在心底里，她是爱他的。「哦，彼得，愿你永远是这样。」她默默祈求。

## XVII / 十七

===Original===
They enjoyed the opera and sang snatches of it coming home as they walked to the subway. Once in the express train, however, Joanna lapsed into sadness.

“I don’t think my voice is as big as that prima donna’s, but those dancing girls! I should have been right up there with them! Oh, Peter, I believe I’m the least bit discouraged.”

She told him of her trips with Bertully. “I didn’t mind those girls calling me ‘nigger.’ That was sheer ill-breeding. Remember what we used to say when we were children when they called us names?” She recited it: “ ‘Sticks and stones may break my bones, but names will never hurt me.’ What I minded was that they couldn’t dream of my being accepted. Thought I had a nerve even to ask it.”

She mounted the steps. “Come in, Peter.”
===Chinese===
这场歌剧两人看得尽兴，回家的路上朝地铁站走去时，还哼唱着剧中的片段。然而一坐上快车，乔安娜便黯然神伤起来。

「我看我的嗓子是不如那位头牌女主角的洪亮，可那些跳舞的姑娘们呢！我本该就跟她们一起站在台上！唉，彼得，我怕是有点泄气了。」

她跟他讲起随贝图利四处奔走的那些日子。「那些姑娘管我叫『黑鬼』，我倒不往心里去——那纯粹是没有教养。还记得咱们小时候，人家骂咱们，咱们总说的那句话吗？」她背了起来：「『棍棒石头或许打断我的骨头，恶语却永远伤不了我。』我在意的，是她们压根想不到我竟会被接纳，觉得我连开这个口都是胆大妄为。」

她登上台阶。「进来吧，彼得。」

===Original===
After dinner they sat in the back parlor and Joanna went on with her story, Peter listening closely.

“I’m glad you’re telling me about this, Joanna,” he said seriously. “Now you’ll understand my case better. You know how I feel about white people and their everlasting unfairness. As though the world and all that in it is belonged to them! I tell you, Jan, I’m sick of the whole business﻿—college, my everlasting grind, my poverty, this confounded prejudice. If I want to get a chance to study a certain case and it’s in a white hospital you’d think I’d committed a crime. As though diseases picked out different races! I’m a good surgeon, I’ll swear I am, but I’ve got so I don’t care whether I get my degree or not. You can’t imagine all the petty unfairness about me. Only the other day the barber refused to shave me in the college barbershop. Your own cousin, John Talbert, is a Zeta Gamma man if ever there was one﻿—that’s the equivalent to Phi Beta Kappa in his school, you know. Do you think he got it? No, they blackballed him out.”

Joanna sat silent, stunned by this avalanche. And to think she had precipitated it!
===Chinese===
晚饭后，两人坐在后客厅里，乔安娜接着讲她的经历，彼得凝神听着。

「乔安娜，你肯把这些讲给我听，我很高兴，」他正色道，「这下你能更明白我的处境了。你知道我对白人、对他们那没完没了的不公平是什么感受。好像这个世界和世上的一切都归他们所有似的！我告诉你，小乔，这一切我都烦透了——大学、无休无止的苦熬、我的穷日子、这该死的偏见。我想寻个机会研究某个病例，只要那病人在白人医院里，那架势就仿佛我犯了什么罪。好像疾病还会挑选种族似的！我是个好外科医生，我敢发誓，可我已经弄到连学位拿得成拿不成都无所谓的地步。围绕着我的种种琐碎不公，你根本无从想象。就在前几天，学院理发店里的理发师还拒绝给我刮脸。你自己的表哥约翰·塔尔伯特（John Talbert），论资格是十足的泽塔·伽马（Zeta Gamma）会员——你知道，在他那所学校，那就相当于优等生荣誉学会。你以为他到手了吗？没有，人家投黑球把他刷了出去。」

乔安娜默默坐着，被这一番倾泻震得说不出话来。想不到这竟是她引出来的！

===Original===
“Arabelle Morton’s sister, Selma,” Peter went on morosely, “took her Master’s degree last year. The candidates sat in alphabetical order. Selma sat in her seat wondering whom the chair on the left of her belonged to﻿—it was vacant. At the last moment a girl came in, a Miss Nelson, who had been in one or two of her classes. Selma knew she was a Southerner. ‘Oh, I just can’t sit there,’ Selma heard her say, not too much under her breath. And some friend of hers went to the Professor in charge of the exercises and he let her change her place, though it threw the whole line out of order.”

He paused, still brooding.

“Another colored girl﻿—can’t think of her name﻿—paid for a seat in one of the Seminary rooms. The white girl next to her, apparently a very pleasant person, had her books all over her own desk space and this one, too. They were the best seats in the room. The colored girl asked her to move them. She just looked at her. Then this Miss﻿—Miss Taylor, that was her name, took it from one authority to another, finally to the professor in charge of the Library. He assigned her another seat. Said the girl had been there four years, and that anyway, she﻿—the white girl﻿—resented the colored girl’s manner toward her. The damned petty injustice!”
===Chinese===
「阿拉贝尔·莫顿（Arabelle Morton）的妹妹塞尔玛（Selma），」彼得郁郁地接着说，「去年拿硕士学位。应考的人按姓氏字母顺序入座。塞尔玛坐在自己的位子上，心里纳闷左手边那把椅子是谁的——一直空着。最后一刻进来一个姑娘，是位纳尔逊小姐（Nelson），跟她同过一两门课。塞尔玛知道她是南方人。『噢，我可坐不了那儿，』塞尔玛听见她说，声音压得并不怎么低。她的哪个朋友便去找主持典礼的教授，教授竟准她换了座位，尽管这样一换，整个座次全乱了套。」

他顿住不说了，兀自郁郁出神。

「还有一个有色人种姑娘——想不起她叫什么了——在神学院的一间教室里花钱订了个座位。她旁边的白人姑娘，瞧着是个挺和气的人，却把书摊满了自己的桌面，还摊到这个座位上来。那可是全教室最好的座位。有色人种姑娘请她把书挪开。她只是直直地瞪着人家。于是这位小姐——泰勒小姐（Taylor），对了，是这个名字——把这事从一个主管那里闹到另一个主管那里，最后闹到主管图书馆的教授跟前。教授给她另派了个座位。说那姑娘在那儿坐了四年了，还说什么不管怎样，她——那白人姑娘——看不惯那有色人种姑娘对她的态度。这鸡毛蒜皮的混账不公！」

===Original===
“But, Peter,” Joanna argued, “you wouldn’t let that interfere with your whole career, change your whole life?”

“Why shouldn’t I? There’re plenty of pleasanter ways to earn a living. Why should I take any more of their selfish dog-in-the-manger foolishness? I can make all the money I want with Tom Mason. If you aren’t satisfied for me to be an accompanist, I could go into partnership with him and we could form and place orchestras. It’s a perfectly feasible plan, Joanna. Why shouldn’t I pick the job that comes handiest, since the world owes me a living?”

He frowned, meditating. “Isn’t it funny, I felt just then as though I’d been through all this before. It’s just as though I’d heard myself say that very thing some other time. Well, what do you say, Joanna?”
===Chinese===
「可是，彼得，」乔安娜争辩道，「你总不至于让这种事搅了你的整个前程，改了你的一生吧？」

「为什么不至于？谋生的路子多的是，比这舒坦的也有的是。他们那种自私自利、狗占马槽的蠢行，我凭什么还要再受下去？跟汤姆·梅森搭伙，我想要多少钱都挣得来。要是你不乐意我当伴奏，我可以同他合伙，组办乐队、安排到各处演出。这计划完全行得通，乔安娜。既然这世界欠着我一口饭吃，我干吗不挑个最顺手的事由？」

他皱起眉头，沉吟着。「怪不怪，方才那一会儿，我觉得这一切我好像全经历过，就好像从前什么时候，我听见自己说过一模一样的话。唉，你说呢，乔安娜？」

===Original===
“That I don’t want a coward and a shirker for a husband. As though that weren’t the thing those white people﻿—those mean ones﻿—wanted! Not all white people are that way. Both of us know it, Peter. And it’s up to us, to you and me, Peter Bye, to show them we can stick to our last as well as anybody else. If they can take the time to be petty, we can take the time to walk past it. Oh, we must fight it when we can, but we mustn’t let it hold us back. Buck up, Peter, be a man. You’ve got to be one if you’re going to marry me.”

He shrugged his shoulders. “May I light a cigarette?” But she noticed he did it with trembling fingers. “Just as you say, Joanna.”

She rose and faced him, this new Peter﻿—this old Peter if she did but know it, with the early shiftlessness, the irresoluteness of his father, Meriwether Bye, the ancient grudge of his grandfather, Isaiah Bye, rearing up, bearing full and perfect fruit in his heart. Both rage and despair possessed her, as she saw the beautiful fabric of their future felled wantonly to the ground. For the sake of a few narrow pedants!

“Peter, Peter, we’ve got to make our own lives. We can’t let these people ruin us.” She felt her knees trembling under her. “We’re both tired and beside ourselves. Come and see me tomorrow, will you?”
===Chinese===
「我说的是，我不想让一个胆小鬼、一个临阵脱逃的人做丈夫。那不恰恰是那些白人——那些刻薄的白人——巴望的事吗！白人并不都是那号人，这你我都知道，彼得。而该由我们，由你和我，彼得·拜，来让他们瞧瞧：我们也能像任何人一样守住各自的本分。他们有工夫使小心眼，我们就有工夫从旁边走过去。唉，斗得过的场合我们自然要斗，可绝不能让它拖住我们的脚步。振作起来，彼得，做个男子汉。你要想娶我，就得先拿出男子汉的样子。」

他耸了耸肩膀。「我能抽支烟吗？」但她注意到，他是用颤抖的手指点的烟。「就照你说的办吧，乔安娜。」

她站起身，面对着他，面对着这个陌生的彼得——她若细想便会明白，这正是那个旧日的彼得：父亲梅里韦瑟·拜年轻时的懒散和优柔寡断，祖父艾赛亚·拜的积年宿怨，此刻全在他心里抬起头来，结出完满成熟的果实。眼看着两人未来的锦绣图景被恣意砍倒在地，愤怒与绝望一齐攫住了她。就为了几个心胸狭隘的学究！

「彼得，彼得，我们的日子得自己挣出来，不能让这些人毁了咱们。」她觉得双膝在身下打颤。「咱们俩都累了，都失了常态。明天再来看我，好吗？」

===Original===
What should she say to him now, she wondered next day after a long white night. And once she had only to raise her finger and he was willing, glad to do her bidding. Could it be that after all these years she had failed to touch his pride, worse yet that he had no pride? She had been longing so for a cessation from all this bickering, so that they might have time for a touch of tenderness. But she could not afford that now. His love for her was her strongest hold over him. She was sure she could bring him back to reason. Perhaps she had been a little severe last night, calling him a coward.

“I musn’t lose my temper,” she told herself. Yet that was the very thing she did. The matter took such a sudden, such a grotesque turn.

He came in about eleven, his handsome face haggard, his eyes bloodshot. She was astounded at his appearance.

“Peter, you look dreadful!”
===Chinese===
熬过整整一夜的辗转难眠，第二天她暗自思量，如今该对他说什么。想当初，她只消抬一抬手指，他便心甘情愿、欢欢喜喜地听凭差遣。莫非这么多年下来，她竟没能触动他的自尊心？更糟的是，莫非他压根就没有自尊心？她一直多么盼望这一切口角争闹能停一停，好让两人有工夫温存片刻。可眼下她顾不上这个了。他对她的爱，是她攥住他的最有力的一手。她确信自己能引他回心转意、重归理性。也许昨晚骂他胆小鬼，是严厉了些。

「我千万别发脾气。」她对自己说。可她偏偏就发了。事情来了个那样突然、那样荒唐的转折。

他十一点来钟到了，英俊的面容憔悴不堪，两眼布满血丝。他的模样把她吓了一跳。

「彼得，你这样子糟透了！」

===Original===
He glanced over the top of her head at his reflection in the mirror, lounged to the sofa, threw himself in the corner of it.

“Guess I’m due to look a fright after staying up all night. Didn’t get to bed till five this morning.”

She thought he’d been worrying over their quarrel. “You poor boy, you didn’t need to take it that hard.”

He stared at her. “Take what, that hard? Oh, our talk! That didn’t keep me awake. I spent the night at ‘Jake’s.’ ”
===Chinese===
他的目光越过她的头顶，朝镜子里自己的影子瞥了一眼，懒洋洋地踱到沙发跟前，一屁股陷进沙发角里。

「熬了个通宵，人吓人也是活该。今儿早上五点才摸着床。」

她还当他为两人的争执熬了一夜。「你这可怜的人儿，犯不着把那事想得那么重。」

他直勾勾地望着她。「把什么想得那么重？哦，咱们那番话呀！那可没叫我睡不着。我在『杰克』（Jake's）那儿过的夜。」

===Original===
“Jake’s” was the cabaret, a cheap one, in which he had played years ago.

She couldn’t understand him. “I thought you had plenty of money without playing there.”

“I have. I didn’t play there. I was a visitor like anybody else, like Harry Portor; he spent the night there, too. There was a whole gang of us.”

Clearly she must get to the bottom of this. While she had been tossing sleepless, he had been in a cabaret, dancing with cheap women, laughing, drinking perhaps.
===Chinese===
「杰克」就是那家卡巴莱，档次很低的一家，他多年前曾在那里演奏过。

她弄不懂他了。「我还以为你不缺钱花，用不着去那儿演奏呢。」

「我是不缺。我没在那儿演奏。我就是个客人，跟旁人一样，跟哈里·波托尔一样；他也在那儿待了一宿。我们一大帮子人。」

显然，这事她必须查个水落石出。她在这里翻来覆去睡不着的时候，他却待在卡巴莱里，搂着轻浮的女人跳舞，说说笑笑，兴许还喝着酒。

===Original===
“You mean you deliberately went there to have a good time and stayed all night? You and Harry Portor and the rest drank, I suppose?”

“I don’t think Portor did. He’s a full-fledged doctor now, though he’s hardly any practice yet. But the rest of us did. There’s nothing in that, Joanna, fellow’s got to get to know the world.”

Her anger rose, broke. She lost her dignity.

“I suppose Maggie Ellersley taught you that, too.”
===Chinese===
「你是说，你存心上那儿寻欢作乐，还待了一整夜？你跟哈里·波托尔，还有其余那些人，都喝了酒，我猜得对不对？」

「波托尔大概没喝。他如今是正牌医生了，虽说还没多少就诊的病人。可我们其余的人是喝了。这有什么了不得，乔安娜，小伙子总得见识见识世面。」

她的怒火腾起来，炸开了。她顾不得体面了。

「我猜，这也是玛吉·埃勒斯利教你的吧。」

===Original===
“What’s that?” His handsome face lowered. “Say, how’d Maggie Ellersley get into this? No, she never taught me anything. But I can tell you what, if a fellow were going with her and went during his holidays to have a spree at a cabaret she wouldn’t nag him about it, like you nag me. Yes, about that and about a thousand other things.”

She turned into ice. “I’ll never nag you again. Here, take this thing!” She drew off the little ring. “I don’t want it.”

A pin dropping would have crashed in that silence.

His voice came back to him. “You don’t mean this, Joanna﻿—you can’t.”
===Chinese===
「你说什么？」他那英俊的脸沉了下来。「哎，玛吉·埃勒斯利怎么扯到这里头来了？不，她从没教过我什么。不过我告诉你，一个小伙子要是跟她处对象，假期里去卡巴莱痛快一场，她可不会像你数落我这样去数落他。对，这事不数落，一千桩别的事也不数落。」

她整个人化成了冰。「我再也不数落你了。喏，把这东西拿去！」她褪下那枚小小的戒指。「我不要了。」

那样的一片寂静里，一根别针落地也会轰然作响。

他这才找回自己的声音。「你不是当真的，乔安娜——你不可能是当真的。」

===Original===
“I do. Here, take it.”

“You﻿—you mean the engagement is broken?” He ignored her outstretched hand.

She dropped the ring in his pocket. “I mean I can’t consider a man for a husband who throws away his career because of the meanness of a few white men. Of a man who sits all night in a low cabaret where every loafer in New York can point him out and say, ‘That’s the kind of fellow Joanna Marshall goes about with.’ ”

“Oh, I see, it isn’t for my sweet sake, then!”

She pushed him toward the door. “Go, Peter! Go!”
===Chinese===
「我是当真的。喏，拿去。」

「你——你是说婚约吹了？」他不理会她伸出的手。

她把戒指丢进他的口袋。「我是说，一个只因几个白人刻薄就扔掉前程的男人，我没法考虑让他做丈夫。一个整夜坐在下等卡巴莱里的男人，我也没法考虑——在那儿，纽约随便哪个游手好闲的家伙都能指着他，说：『跟乔安娜·马歇尔来往的，就是这么号人。』」

「哦，我懂了，敢情不是为了心疼我呀！」

她把他朝门口推去。「走吧，彼得！走吧！」

===Original===
On New Year’s morning he came back, humble, contrite. “I was a fool, Joanna. I must have been mad. Please forgive me.”

“Of course I do, Peter.”

He fumbled in his pocket, held out the ring. “Will you take this back?”

“I can’t do that.”
===Chinese===
元旦清晨，他回来了，低声下气，满怀悔意。「我是个蠢货，乔安娜。我准是疯了。请你原谅我。」

「我当然原谅你，彼得。」

他在口袋里摸索了一阵，掏出戒指。「你能把它收回去吗？」

「这个我不能。」

===Original===
“When will you?”

“I don’t know if ever.”

There was a long silence. He came over and put his hand on the back of her chair, afraid to touch her.

“Joanna, I don’t deserve your love. But you still do love me?”

She nodded slowly.
===Chinese===
「那要等到什么时候？」

「我不知道还有没有那一天。」

一阵长久的沉默。他走过来，把手搭在她椅背上，不敢碰她。

「乔安娜，我配不上你的爱。可你仍然爱我，是吗？」

她缓缓点了点头。

===Original===
His face brightened at that. “But you won’t take back the ring?”

“No, Peter, I can’t take back the ring.”

He knelt and kissed her hands.

“Goodbye, sweetheart, I must go to Philadelphia today. Happy New Year, Joanna.”
===Chinese===
听了这话，他的脸开朗起来。「可你不肯收回戒指？」

「不，彼得，这戒指我收不回来。」

他跪下来，吻了吻她的双手。

「再见了，亲爱的，我今天就得去费城。新年快乐，乔安娜。」

===Original===
She let him go then. None of their other partings had ever been like this. Safe in her room she cried herself sick. “Oh, Peter,” she murmured to herself, “come back like the boy I used to know.” She wished now that she had been easier with him.

“And yet if I were, he’d let go entirely. Well, it must come out all right.” But her heart was heavy.

The very next day she got a letter. Peter must have written her as soon as he arrived in Philadelphia.

“Joanna, I was wrong,” he had written contritely, “I confess had got away somewhat from your manner of thinking, and I suppose I was a little sore, too﻿—your life seems so full. Sometimes I think there is nothing I can bring you. But I do love you, Joanna. You must always believe that and I think you love me, too. We were meant for each other. I am sure life would hold for us the deepest, most irremediable sorrow if we separated. Whether we are engaged or not, just tell me that you love me still and I can be happy.”
===Chinese===
那一次，她由他去了。两人从前所有的分别，都不曾像这一次。回到自己房里，安然关上门，她哭得肝肠寸断。「唉，彼得，」她喃喃自语，「回来吧，变回我从前认得的那个少年。」她如今但愿，方才自己待他能温和些。

「可话说回来，我若是真温和了，他就要彻底撒手了。唉，总会有个圆满结果的吧。」但她的心头沉甸甸的。

就在第二天，她收到一封信。彼得准是一到费城就提笔给她写的。

「乔安娜，我错了，」他满心悔疚地写道，「我承认，我是有些偏离了你的想法，我想我也带着点怨气——你的生活显得那么充实。有时候我觉得，我没有什么可以带给你的。可我真的爱你，乔安娜。你要求远相信这一点，而我想，你也爱我。我们是命中注定的一对。我确信，我们若是分开，人生留给我们的将是至深的、无可挽回的悲哀。不管我们订婚不订婚，只要告诉我你仍然爱我，我就能快乐。」

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===
If she had only answered the letter, then, that very moment!

But she had said to her impulse: “No, I must wait. I can’t let him off too easily.” Perhaps, too, there was a little sense of satisfaction at having him again at her knees, suing for her favors, but this was secondary. Joanna was really sick at heart to think that her beautiful dreams of success for both of them might not be realized. She wanted to be great herself, but she did not want that greatness to overshadow Peter.

Somehow the week slipped by, quickly enough, too. There was always plenty to do. Love﻿—the desire to give it and receive it was tugging persistently at the cords of her being, but she had been too long the slave of Ambition to listen consciously to that. Yet she found herself lying awake nights thinking, thinking, more about Peter than about her singing engagements during the New Year, or about her plan to make her mightiest efforts just now to enter the dancing world. Yet whatever she might ponder by night, she spent all her time and strength by day going to see performances, practicing, inventing new steps and new rhythms.

Through Helena Arnold and indirectly through Vera Sharples she obtained the promise of an interview with one of the season’s favorites.

===Chinese===
她当时要是立刻回了那封信，就在那一刻，该有多好！

可她当时对自己的冲动说：「不行，我得等等。不能这么轻易放过他。」兴许，里头还掺着一点小小的快意——又见他伏在自己膝下、苦苦乞求她的恩宠了——不过这是次要的。一想到她为他们俩织就的那幅双双成名的好梦可能落空，乔安娜心里就真的发苦。她自己要成大器，可她不要那份光辉盖过彼得。

不知怎么，一个星期就滑了过去，倒也过得够快。要办的事总不缺。爱——那给予爱、领受爱的渴望，正执拗地牵扯着她生命之弦，可她给「雄心」当奴隶当了太久，意识上不肯去谛听。然而她发觉自己夜里睁着眼躺着，想了又想，想的更多的是彼得，而不是新年的演唱合约，也不是眼下要使出浑身解数打入舞蹈界的计划。任她夜里千思万想，白天她仍旧把全部时间与力气花在看演出、练功、编新舞步、创新节奏上。

通过海伦娜·阿诺德，又辗转通过维拉·夏普尔斯（Vera Sharples），她求得了一个面谈的许诺，对方是本演出季当红的红人之一。

===Original===
“I’ll be able to see you early Thursday evening,” the famous woman wrote. “You may expect either a note or a telephone call from me.” At one time such a promise would have sent Joanna into the seventh ecstasy, without impairing her confidence. But recent discouragements, persistent﻿—and for her unusual, phenomena﻿—had rendered her timid. She was nervous. Her assurance wavered. She spent the whole day going through her repertory. Sometimes she danced like a maenad. Then she adopted a slow Greek rhythm, posturing and undulating. She struck attitudes before the mirror, standing in one position for long moments.

“For Heaven’s sake,” said Sylvia, putting her head inside the door on one of these occasions, “go out and take a walk, Joanna.” She was as nervous as her sister.

“Not a bad idea, Sylvia, I believe I will. You can answer the phone. Have you seen my brown cape?”

She came back a little after five, refreshed and soothed.

===Chinese===
「星期四傍晚早些时候，我能见你。」那位大名鼎鼎的女人来信写道，「你可以等我一张便条，或是等我一个电话。」搁在从前，这样一个许诺准会叫乔安娜直上七重天，而信心毫发无损。可近来接连的挫败——种种缠磨不休、在她身上又殊为罕见的现象——叫她胆怯了。她心神不定。底气摇摇欲坠。她把一整天都花在温习自己的保留剧目上。有时她跳得像酒神狂女（maenad）。忽而又换上一支缓慢的希腊节拍，摆造型、扭身段，如波起伏。她在镜前摆出种种姿势，一个姿势一站就是老半天。

「看在老天的份上，」有一回西尔维娅把脑袋探进门来，说道，「出去散散步吧，乔安娜。」她自己同妹妹一样心神不宁。

「这主意不坏，西尔维娅，我看就这么办。电话你来接。见着我那件棕色斗篷了吗？」

五点刚过她就回来了，神清气爽，心绪也平顺了。

===Original===
“No phone message,” Sylvia told her, “but here’s a note. What’s she got to say, Janna?” She came and looked over her sister’s arm.

“So sorry not to be able to see you tonight,” the noted artiste had written. “I’m halfway expecting an old friend of mine and must keep the evening free. I shall try to arrange to have you call, just the same, not this month I’m afraid, but certainly in February.” She ended with a meaningless expression of “good wishes.”

“Mercy,” said Sylvia, “why didn’t she say next year?”

Joanna was bitter. “Or next eternity? Sylvia, I wonder if I’m not a darn fool!” She walked upstairs trailing her long brown cape after her.

===Chinese===
「没有电话，」西尔维娅告诉她，「倒是来了张便条。她有什么说的，小乔？」她走过来，从姐姐臂后探头看信。

「万分抱歉，今晚不能见你了。」那位鼎鼎大名的名角（artiste）写道，「我有位老朋友多半要来，这个晚上必须给它空出来。不过我仍会设法安排你来访——恐怕赶不上这个月了，但二月里一定。」末了她照例缀上一句全无意义的「祝好」。

「天哪，」西尔维娅说，「她怎么不干脆说明年？」

乔安娜心里发苦。「要不干脆说来世？西尔维娅，我瞧我八成是个不折不扣的大傻瓜！」她身后拖着长长的棕色斗篷，上楼去了。

===Original===
All her life she had known and seen success. When she was born her father was a successful caterer, almost a wealthy man. It is true that she had seen her own people hindered, checked on account of color, but hardly any of the things she had greatly wanted had been affected for that cause. She had had money enough to have her dancing and music lessons﻿—the very fact that she had had to take separate and special lessons from Bertully meant to her that some special and separate way would be arranged whereby she would become a dancer on the stage.

She did not know how to envisage disappointment.

Strangely enough, the defection of the artiste struck home to her more keenly than the reception which she had had from the stage-managers. She refused Sylvia’s invitation to come back downstairs and spend the evening with her and Brian.

“We might go to a movie,” Sylvia had said tentatively. But Joanna had only made an impatient gesture of refusal, and walking into her room had closed the door very carefully after her.

She did not cry or throw herself across the bed. It might have been better for her if she had. Joanna’s creed was that one kept a stiff upper lip even to oneself. She had not had many occasions to try out that creed.

There she sat, stiffly, on the spindling chair in front of her small flat-topped writing desk and brooded over the future which suddenly stretched dull, stale, and uninvigorating before her. She would never be able to stand it.

===Chinese===
她这辈子，成功是识得的，也是见惯的。她出生时，父亲已是个发迹的宴席承办商，差不多算得上富人。不错，她也见过自己人因肤色的缘故受阻、受挫，可她真正渴求的东西，几乎没有一样是栽在这上头的。她学舞蹈、学音乐，钱从没短缺过——就连她不得不在贝图利那儿另开小灶、单授专课这一层，在她看来也意味着：自会有一道特别的、单独的路子给她铺排出来，好教她登上舞台，做个舞蹈家。

她不知道该怎样去想象「失意」这回事。

说来奇怪，那位名角的背弃，倒比舞台经理们给她的那些冷遇更刺进她心里。西尔维娅邀她回楼下来，同她和布莱恩一起消磨这个晚上，她谢绝了。

「咱们可以去看场电影。」西尔维娅曾试着这样提议。可乔安娜只不耐烦地摆了个拒绝的手势，径直走进自己房里，又小心翼翼地随手把门关严。

她没有哭，也没有扑倒在床上。若是哭了，兴许倒于她有益。乔安娜的信条是：哪怕对自己，也得咬紧牙关不露怯。只是这信条，她还难得有机会践行。

她就那样直挺挺地坐着，坐在那张平顶小书桌前的细腿椅上，闷想那个骤然铺展在眼前的前程——晦暗、陈腐、没有半点生气。这样的光景她一天也受不住。

===Original===
The thought of her marriage flashed across her mind.

“And Peter,” she said to herself aloud, “willing to be ordinary and second-rate! Where is that letter of his? I might just as well answer it now as at any other time.”

In spite of her ugly mood a little wave of tenderness welled up in her heart as she read﻿—“Just tell me that you do love me still﻿—”

“Oh, Peter, Peter,” she murmured, “if I tell you that you’ll never change, never push on. If only you could be strong and let me bring my troubles to you.”

It would never do to let him know how completely she was discouraged. And equally she could not let him know how dear, weakness and all, he was to her. She would make her love conditional. “If you want me to love you, Peter﻿—”

She hated that, but some day they would both be glad of it. She actually cried for the two of them as she wrote her stern little fiction:

===Chinese===
结婚的念头从她心头一闪而过。

「还有彼得，」她出声自言自语，「竟甘心普普通通、做个二流人物！他那封信搁在哪儿？横竖早晚都要回，不如趁现在回了。」

尽管心绪恶劣，读到那一句——「只消告诉我，你确实还爱着我——」——一小股柔情仍从心底涌了上来。

「哦，彼得，彼得，」她喃喃道，「我若照实说了，你就永远不会改变，永远不会奋进了。你要是能坚强起来，让我把烦恼都带到你跟前，该有多好。」

万万不能让他知道，她的心气已经涣散到了什么地步。同样，也万万不能让他知道，他——连同他的软弱——在她心里何等亲爱。她要给她的爱附上条件。「你要是要我爱你，彼得——」

她恨这一手，可将来总有一天，他们俩都会为此庆幸。她当真为他们两个落了泪，一面写下那封板着面孔的小小谎信：

===Original===
> “Dear Peter:
> “No, I don’t love you as you are. The man I marry must be a man worth while like my father or Philip. I couldn’t stand the thought of spending my life with someone ordinary.
> “But I want to love you, Peter. Write me soon and say you are going to get to work in earnest. Happy New Year.
> “Sincerely, 

 “**Joanna**.”

===Chinese===
> 「亲爱的彼得：
> 「不，照你现在这样，我不爱你。我要嫁的男人，必须是个像我父亲或菲利普那样有出息的人。一想到要同一个凡庸之辈厮守一生，我就受不了。
> 「可是我想爱你，彼得。快些来信，说你就要发愤用功、大干起来。新年快乐。
> 「谨启，

 「**乔安娜**。」

===Original===
She read it over and over, totally blind to its supreme egotism. Then she sealed it and, sniffling a little﻿—more like a child than like an artist﻿—went to bed.

In the morning she awoke with a sense of impending disaster. The phrase is trite but so, alas, is disaster. At first, as she lay there, her slender brown arms stretched above her tumbled head, she mused to herself about it.

“Let’s see why I do feel so rotten? What’s the matter?”

She remembered her engagement with the artiste. “But that’s not what’s making me sick,” she told herself after a momentary probing of her self-consciousness. Then recalling the letter to Peter, she got up and walked barefooted across the room to the desk, shivering a little as the chilly January morning air struck at her, billowing her thin nightdress. She thought she would read it again, but the envelope was sealed. It slipped out of her hand and she ran back to bed again, cuddling luxuriously.

===Chinese===
她读了一遍又一遍，对信里那份登峰造极的自私全然视而不见。然后封上信封，抽了抽鼻子——那样子更像孩子，不像艺术家——上床睡了。

早晨醒来，她心头压着大祸将临的预感。这话说得俗套，可不幸，灾祸本身也俗套。起初她还躺着，棕色纤细的手臂伸在乱蓬蓬的头顶上，独自琢磨着这份感觉。

「我倒要想想，我怎么就这么难受？到底是怎么回事？」

她想起与那位名角的约见。「可叫我难受的并不是这个。」她向自己心底探询了片刻，这样告诉自己。随即想起写给彼得的那封信，她便起身，赤着脚穿过房间走到书桌前。一月清晨的寒气向她袭来，吹得薄薄的睡衣鼓起来，她微微打了个寒噤。她本想再读一遍那封信，可信封已经封了口。信从手里滑脱，她又跑回床上，舒舒服服地蜷作一团。

===Original===
“Oh, well!” Afterwards when she rose and closed the windows she promised herself: “If I do send it I’ll write him a sweet, sweet letter soon.”

After breakfast she posted it. It fell with a heaviness into the box that made her uneasy. “I’ll write him again tonight,” she thought. “Poor Peter! He’ll be disappointed, I suppose.”

But the night brought her several offers to sing in Southern schools which she thought she might just as well accept. Apparently nothing was to come of her dancing. She had about a week in which to get ready.

Just before she left, a little surprised that she had not already heard from Peter, she wrote him a long letter, her first long love-letter.

===Chinese===
「唉，算了！」后来起身关窗时，她对自己许诺：「这信若是真寄出去了，我不久就再给他写一封甜甜的、甜甜的信。」

早饭后她把它投进了邮筒。信沉甸甸地落进筒里，落得她心里发慌。「今晚我再给他写一封，」她想，「可怜的彼得！他准会失望的，我猜。」

可当天晚上，好几份邀约找上门来，请她去南方各学校演唱，她想索性应下了也罢。她的舞蹈，眼见得是要一场空了。她大约还有一星期的工夫准备。

临动身前，她有点诧异竟还没得到彼得的音信，便给他写了一封长信——她的第一封长篇情书。

===Original===
> “**Dearest Peter:** [she began]
> “You can’t think how awfully I want to see you. If you were here tonight I shouldn’t quarrel with you one moment.”

She quoted lines from one of Goethe’s poems.

===Chinese===
> 「**最亲爱的彼得**：〔她这样起头〕
> 「你想不到我心里多么、多么想见你。今夜你若在这里，我一秒钟也不会同你拌嘴。」

她引了歌德（Goethe）一首诗里的几行。

===Original===
> “Ein blick von deinen Augen in die meinen,
 

 Ein Kuss von deinen Mund auf meinem Munde:

She hesitated a moment, a little aghast at this disclosure of her feelings. “But I might just as well, he deserves it. Dear, dear Peter, if I could just see you!”

She ended, smiling shamefacedly at her own abandon﻿—

> “Mein einzig Glück auf Erden ist dein Wille”﻿—

===Chinese===
> 「你双眸的一瞥，投进我的眼底，

 你唇上的一个吻，印上我的嘴唇：

她迟疑了片刻，这样袒露心迹，叫她自己微微心惊。「可索性写了也好，他受得起。亲爱的、亲爱的彼得，要是能见上你一面就好了！」

她这样收尾，为自己这番忘情赧然一笑——

> 「我在这人世唯一的幸福，就是你的意愿」——

===Original===
She might have stopped in Philadelphia on her way South, but she couldn’t after that letter. In Richmond she received a note from Peter which Sylvia had forwarded.

> “**My dear Joanna:** [she was surprised at the formality]
> “I have both your letters. I cannot tell you how surprised I was at receiving the first or how much I cherished the second. Joanna, I would give ten years of my life if you had written the second one first. I am very busy now but I am going to write you a final letter very soon.
> “Sincerely, 

 “**Peter**.”

===Chinese===
南下的路上，她本可以在费城停一停，可写出了那样一封信，她停不得了。到了里士满，她收到一封彼得的信，是西尔维娅转寄来的。

> 「**亲爱的乔安娜**：〔这个一本正经的称呼叫她吃了一惊〕
> 「你的两封信都收到了。收到第一封时我是何等惊讶、第二封又被我何等珍爱，我说不上来。乔安娜，倘若你先写来的是第二封，我情愿拿十年寿命去换。眼下我很忙，但我不久就会给你写一封最后的信。
> 「谨启，

 「**彼得**。」

===Original===
“ ‘A final letter,’ ” she quoted to herself. “What a funny thing to say! Oh, Peter! And I wanted, I needed a real letter, a love-letter!” Her natural reasonableness helped her. “It’s my own fault. I suppose he feels like I feel sometimes, don’t-care-y. But ‘a final letter.’ I wonder what he meant!”

But she did not puzzle long. Richmond was appreciative and gay. Someone wrote her from Hampton and asked her to do an interpretative dance. Partly because of the interest and excitement, partly because she had forced herself to do so often, she resolutely put Peter out of her mind.

“He’ll know when I write him again,” she told herself ruefully.

===Chinese===
「『一封最后的信』，」她在心里默念。「这话多古怪！哦，彼得！我要的是、我缺的是一封真正的信，一封情书啊！」她天生的那份通情达理帮了她。「这是我自己招的。我猜他这会儿的心境，就跟我有时候一样——什么都懒得上心了。可是『一封最后的信』。他到底什么意思！」

可她没有为此纳闷多久。里士满待她热诚，日子也快活。有人从汉普顿（Hampton）来信，请她跳一支阐释性舞蹈（interpretative dance）。一半因为新鲜热闹，一半因为她早已惯于强逼自己，她毅然把彼得从心里撵了出去。

「等我再给他写信时，他就会明白了。」她怅然对自己说。

===Original===
Two weeks, a month passed; she came into her room one day to find a bulky letter from Sylvia. “He doesn’t mean it, Joanna, of course, but I had to send it.” Thus her sister’s note. Puzzled, she read the enclosure, which turned out to be a letter from Peter to Sylvia.

> “Dear Sylvia:
> “I am writing to let you know that I am to be married in June. Joanna told me she didn’t love me and so I am going to marry Maggie Neal; she’s crazy about me. Tell Joanna not to bother sending back any of the things I’ve given her.
> “Sincerely, 

 “**Peter**.”

===Chinese===
两个星期，一个月过去了。一天她走进房间，发现一封西尔维娅寄来的厚厚的信。「他不是当真的，乔安娜，当然不是，可我不得不寄给你。」她姐姐的便条上如此说。她满腹狐疑地读那随信附来的东西，原来是彼得写给西尔维娅的一封信。

> 「亲爱的西尔维娅：
> 「写信告诉你，我六月就要结婚了。乔安娜对我说她不爱我，所以我打算娶玛吉·尼尔；她迷我迷得不得了。告诉乔安娜，不必费心把我送她的东西寄还回来。
> 「谨启，

 「**彼得**。」

## XX / 二十

===Original===
Sylvia had written. “He doesn’t mean it, of course”﻿—

But Joanna knew better. Even while dumbfounded she stood staring at the note, trying to believe there must be some mistake, her heart, her every sense was telling her it was too true.

Peter had given her up. He was going to marry Maggie. *He had given her up.* That was the important thing. For if he was not to marry her, what difference did it make whom he married?

She had never been religious, she had never been dramatic. Rather she somewhat despised any emphatically emotional display. “People don’t really act that way,” she told herself.

===Chinese===
西尔维娅写过信了。「他当然不是当真的」——

可是乔安娜心里更清楚。她目瞪口呆地站在那里，盯着那张便条，竭力想相信这中间必定出了什么差错，而她的心、她的每一种感官都在告诉她：这是千真万确的。

彼得把她放弃了。他要去娶玛吉了。*他把她放弃了。*这才是要紧的事。因为他若娶的不是她，那他娶的是谁又有什么分别呢？

她从来不信教，也从来不做戏；相反，她对任何激烈外露的感情表现都有几分瞧不上。「人不会真的那样行事，」她对自己说。

===Original===
Yet she dropped on her knees beside the pine bedstead in the sparsely furnished room. Her hands clutched at the counterpane. She could feel her throat constricting. A scalding hotness seared her nostrils, her mouth became dry, her eyeballs burned.

“Oh, God! Oh, Peter!” She repeated the two phrases again and again in a sick agony.

“God, you couldn’t let it be true. You know I always loved him, I didn’t hide it from you. You knew my heart.”

===Chinese===
然而她还是跪倒在那个陈设简陋的房间里松木床架的旁边。双手紧紧攥住床罩。她觉得喉咙一阵阵收紧。一股滚烫的灼热燎着鼻孔，嘴里发干，两眼灼痛。

「哦，上帝！哦，彼得！」她把这两句话翻来覆去地念着，陷入病态的苦楚之中。

「上帝，你不能让这是真的。你知道我一直爱他，我从没有瞒过你。你是知道我这颗心的。」

===Original===
At first she thought she would go to him. Then the fear that he might not want to see her, might even refuse to see her, overcame her. That humiliation she could never endure.

She sat down and wrote him a long letter, her pen flying over the page like something bewitched. It could not move fast enough to empty her heart of all she had to tell. If she could only make clear to him that she had “chastened” him because she loved him. How patronizing, how silly she had been. She said aloud, “How he and Maggie must have laughed at me, setting myself up above them and their ideas as though I were some goddess! Oh, God, why did you let me do it? You knew what I really meant.”

Her tears almost blotted out her words.

===Chinese===
起初她想去找他。可随即又生出一种恐惧：他也许不愿意见她，甚至可能拒绝见她。这恐惧把她压倒了。那样的羞辱她无论如何也受不了。

她坐下来给他写了一封长信，笔尖在纸页上飞驰，像着了魔一般。它跑得再快，也不够把她心里要倾吐的话倒个干净。她要是能让他明白就好了：她之所以「磨炼」他，正是因为爱他。她那时多么居高临下、多么愚蠢啊。她出声说道：「他和玛吉准是笑话死我了——我把自己抬到他们和他们那些想法的上头，好像我是个什么女神似的！哦，上帝，你为什么让我做出那样的事来？你原是知道我的真意的。」

她的眼泪几乎把字迹都洇没了。

===Original===
The post-office was a mile away but she trudged the distance mechanically, seeing nothing, hearing nothing, absorbed and drowned in the black sorrow which overwhelmed her.

Peter’s answer, which came in four days, brought no solace. She had never dwelt on any pages as she did on those of his last letter. The curt, stern phrases both cut her and awakened a new respect for him.

With a sense of responsibility which Joanna had never seen in him before, he insisted on honoring the claim which Maggie’s complete and unexacting love made upon him. “Even if I wanted to give her up,” he wrote in a sort of anguished virtuousness, “I would not, she has been too kind to me. But I don’t want to give her up, Joanna. Besides, I’ve got to consider the public. She has told several people that we are engaged.”

Joanna cried aloud: “If you had only been like this before, ever before, only once, I’d have known I couldn’t trifle with you. Oh, Peter, you deceived me.” The tears stood, great wells of water about her eyes.

===Chinese===
邮局在一英里开外，可她浑浑噩噩地一步一步走了去，什么也看不见，什么也听不见，整个人沉溺、淹灭在那吞没她的黑色哀伤里。

彼得的回信四天以后到了，没有带来半点安慰。她从来没有像琢磨他这最后一封信的字字句句那样，在任何书页上逗留过。那些简短冷峻的句子既割着她，又唤起她对他的一种新的敬重。

他带着一种乔安娜从未在他身上见过的责任感，坚持要报答玛吉那份全心全意、不求回报的爱加诸他的情分。「就算我想放弃她，」他写道，语气里带着一种痛苦的道义自许，「我也不会那样做，她对我太好了。何况我也并不想放弃她，乔安娜。再说，我还得顾及外界。她已经告诉好几个人，我们订了婚。」

乔安娜放声哭道：「你要是早些像这样，从前任何时候，哪怕只有一回，我也早该明白你不容我轻慢戏弄。哦，彼得，你骗了我。」泪水盈眶，在她眼睛四周积成两大汪水。

===Original===
She finished her engagement in the quiet Southern city before an audience which wondered vaguely what had happened to make Joanna Marshall different. Somehow she packed her trunk, thanked the persistent youth who had constituted himself her cavalier, and boarded the Jim Crow car. Her cavalier for all his persistence had been unable to obtain for her Pullman accommodations. After Washington she fell to wondering what it used to be like in other days, less than a year ago, when she would be coming up this way, through Baltimore, Wilmington, past Chester, secure in the knowledge that Peter would be waiting for her at West Philadelphia. He would never be there again! How could she endure it? It was not possible that anyone could stand this thing. No wonder people “crossed in love”﻿—she dwelt on the phrase distastefully﻿—killed themselves. She toyed with the idea. Of course *she* couldn’t; that sort of relief was not for her. In the first place it was cowardly. With her usual mental clarity she visualized the colored papers of Harlem. There would be notices telling how the “gifted singer, Joanna Marshall, daughter of Joel Marshall, died by her own hand﻿—”

Her mind lingered over it, painting in new details, consciously withdrawing as far as possible from the real cause of her grief.

As the train slid into the long shed at West Philadelphia she pressed her face against the windowpane and strained out into the dusk. Sometimes miracles did occur. Perhaps he was there, perhaps none of it was true. Her tears crept down the glass, the man behind her watching curiously.

===Chinese===
她在南方那座安静的城市里唱完了约定的演出，台下的听众只隐隐纳闷，不知出了什么事，让乔安娜·马歇尔变了个人。她不知怎么总算收拾好了衣箱，谢过那位自告奋勇充当护花使者的执着青年，登上了吉姆·克劳（Jim Crow）隔离车厢。她这位护花使者再执着，也没能替她弄到普尔曼（Pullman）卧铺。过了华盛顿，她不由得想起从前——还不到一年呢——她走这条线北上时的光景：穿过巴尔的摩、威尔明顿，经过切斯特，心里踏踏实实地知道彼得会在西费城等她。他再也不会在那儿了！这教她怎么受得了？这种事谁也不可能受得住。怪不得人们「情场失意」——她嫌恶地咂摸着这个词——就寻了短见。她把那个念头把玩了一会儿。当然，*她*是不能那样的；那种解脱轮不到她。首先，那是懦夫行径。凭着素来的头脑清明，她把哈莱姆那些有色人种报纸的样子想象了出来。那上面会登出消息，说「才华出众的歌唱家乔安娜·马歇尔，乔尔·马歇尔之女，自戕殒命」——

她的心思在这上头流连不去，添绘出新的细节，有意尽可能躲开那桩真正让她伤心的事。

火车滑进西费城长长的棚式站台时，她把脸贴在车窗玻璃上，竭力朝暮色里张望。有时候奇迹确实会发生。也许他就在那儿，也许那一切都不是真的。她的眼泪慢慢爬下玻璃，身后的男人好奇地望着她。

===Original===
Sylvia met her in New York, got her home and finally to bed. Mr. and Mrs. Marshall knew nothing of the matter and Sylvia had told even Brian very little. The two girls said nothing about Peter directly.

“Help me to get to sleep, Sylvia,” Joanna said suddenly after a rambling account of her trip. Her roving eyes and twitching hands had already betrayed her need. “Help me to get to sleep or I think I shall go mad.”

===Chinese===
西尔维娅到纽约接她，把她送回家，最后又安顿她上了床。马歇尔先生和马歇尔太太对这件事一无所知，西尔维娅连对布莱恩都几乎没有说什么。姐妹俩没有直接提起彼得一个字。

「帮我睡着吧，西尔维娅。」乔安娜把这一路的经过东拉西扯讲了一通之后，忽然这么说。她游移不定的眼神和微微抽动的双手早已泄露了她的需要。「帮我睡着吧，不然我怕自己要疯了。」

## XXI / 二十一

===Original===
Joanna was in agony. Her life, hitherto a thing of light and laughter and pleasant work, became a nightmare of regret and morbid introspection. She could not blame herself enough. Nothing that Sylvia could say would make her speak unkindly of Peter.

===Chinese===
乔安娜深陷在痛苦之中。她的生活，本是光明、欢笑与称心工作织成的东西，如今变成了一场悔恨与病态自省交织的噩梦。她怎么责备自己都不嫌够。无论西尔维娅说什么，都无法让她说彼得一句坏话。

===Original===
“No, Sylvia, it wasn’t his fault, really, it was all mine. Of course I think he was a little stupid not to see that my very interest in him, my constant faultfinding grew out of my wish to have him perfect. And I wanted him to be perfect because I loved him. But if I had ever dreamed how much I was hurting him, I’d never have said a word to him. I’d rather have had him exactly as he was, faults and all, than to lose him altogether.”

===Chinese===
「不，西尔维娅，那不是他的错，真的，全都是我的错。当然，我觉得他也有一点儿笨，竟没有看出，我对他那份关切、我那没完没了的挑错，恰恰出于我要他完美的心愿。我要他完美，是因为我爱他。可我要是做梦也想到自己伤他伤得那么深，我就会一个字也不对他说。我宁肯要他原原本本的样子，连缺点带一切，也不肯把他整个儿丢了。」

===Original===
She suffered intensely, too, from wounded pride. “Just think, Sylvia, he didn’t, he couldn’t have loved me after all. He just wanted to get married. See how easily he turned from me. Oh, if I had known that was all he wished, I’d have been different. I’d have been just the kind of woman he wanted.”

===Chinese===
她的自尊受了伤，这也叫她痛苦万分。「你想想，西尔维娅，他并不爱我，他到底是不可能爱过我的。他不过是想结婚罢了。瞧他把我撇下撇得多容易。唉，我要是早知道他要的只是这一点，我就会是另一副样子。我就会恰恰做成他想要的那种女人。」

===Original===
Her humble sincerity almost made Sylvia cry.

Another girl in Joanna’s place might not have suffered so intensely. But Joanna, poor creature, was doomed by her very virtues. That same single-mindedness which had made her so engrossed in her art, now proved her undoing. Her mind, shocked out of its normal complacence, perceived and dwelt on a new aspect of life, an entirely different and undreamed of sense of values. For the first time in her life she saw the importance of human relationships. What did a knowledge of singing, dancing, of any of the arts amount to without people, without parents, brothers, sisters, lovers to share one’s failures, one’s triumphs?

===Chinese===
她这份低首下心的诚恳，几乎叫西尔维娅落下泪来。

换一个女孩处在乔安娜的地位，未必会痛苦得这般厉害。可是乔安娜，这可怜的人，偏偏是叫自己的美德毁掉的。正是那股曾让她一门心思扑在艺术上的痴劲，如今成了她的祸根。她的头脑被惊出了惯常的自满，看出并萦回于人生的一个新面向，一套全然不同、做梦也想不到的价值标准。她生平头一遭看见了人际情分的重要。唱歌、跳舞，任何一门艺术的造诣，若没有他人——没有父母、兄弟、姊妹、恋人来分担自己的失败、分享自己的胜利——又算得了什么呢？

===Original===
She remembered how interested, how faithfully interested all her family had been in her small career. Even Brian Spencer, now that her own brothers were away, felt responsible for her, shifted engagements to get her to the station on time, met trains at ghastly, inconvenient hours of the night. And Peter had been her slave, her willing, unquestioning slave, eager to accomplish any task no matter how troublesome, for a word of appreciation from her.

And without a thought she had taken all this as her due.

===Chinese===
她想起全家人对她那份小小的前程曾如何关切，如何始终不渝地关切。就连布莱恩·斯宾塞，如今她自己的哥哥们都不在身边，也觉得对她负有责任，为了准时送她上车站而挪动别的约会，在深夜那些要命的、不便的钟点里去接车。而彼得，是她的奴仆，一个心甘情愿、从不多问的奴仆，只要换她一句称许，再麻烦的差事也巴不得去办成。

而她呢，想也不曾想过，就把这一切当作了自己应得之物。

===Original===
She had failed to realize happiness when she saw it. The bird had been in her grasp and she had let it go. This was her constant thought. Of course, she still had her own people. And she was considerate of them now, painfully anxious to show her gratitude. She tried to stammer out an apology to Sylvia for her past remissness.

===Chinese===
幸福到了眼前，她却不曾认出来。鸟儿分明已握在她掌中，她却放它飞了。这是她念念不忘的心事。当然，她的亲人还在。她如今也体贴他们了，急切得近乎痛苦地想要表明自己的感激。她试着嗫嗫嚅嚅地向西尔维娅为往日的怠慢道歉。

===Original===
But her sister threw an arm about her and strained her close. “Don’t be so thoughtful, so good, Jan. You break my heart. I’d rather have you your old thoughtless, impatient self.”

===Chinese===
可姐姐一把搂住她，紧紧贴在怀里。「别这么体贴、这么乖，小乔。你把我的心都揉碎了。我宁愿你还是从前那个没心没肺、急性子的你。」

===Original===
Of course, this expression of gratitude was really only a gesture to life, to fate. “If Peter could come back to me now, he’d see how truly I cared about him. God, couldn’t you let him come back?” Joanna, who had hardly uttered a prayer outside of “Now I lay me,” spent most of her thoughts at this time in communion with God﻿—“You Great Power, you great force, you whatever it is that rules things.” Walking, riding, any action at all mechanical she utilized in concentrating on her “desire to have everything come right.”

===Chinese===
当然，这份感激的表露，其实不过是向生活、向命运做出的一个姿态。「要是彼得这会儿能回到我身边，他就会看出我是多么真心地在乎他。上帝啊，你就不能让他回来吗？」乔安娜除了「如今我躺下安睡」（Now I lay me）以外，几乎从没念过一句祷词，这时却把大半心思都用在与神相交上了——「你这伟大的权能，你这浩大的伟力，你，不管你是什么，总归是主宰万事的那一位。」走路、坐车，凡是一切机械不费心的动作，她都拿来凝神专注于自己那个「盼一切好转的心愿」。

===Original===
In the mornings, weak and spent with the wakefulness of her white night, she picked up her little slim Bible and read portions of the Psalms. The beautiful words not only soothed her but brought with them a wonderment at the passion and pain which they revealed. “David, you, too, suffered. Help me, help me now.” So intense was her thought that she would hardly have been surprised if she had looked up and seen the Psalmist bending over her.

===Chinese===
清晨，熬过整夜无眠，虚弱而乏力，她拿起自己那本小巧的薄圣经，读上几段《诗篇》。那些美丽的字句不仅抚慰了她，还带来一阵惊叹，惊叹其间流露的激情与苦痛。「大卫啊，你也曾受苦。帮帮我，现在就帮帮我。」她的意念炽烈到这般地步，倘若一抬头看见《诗篇》的作者正俯身望着自己，她恐怕也不会吃惊。

===Original===
She hated the mornings even more than the nights. In spite of her wakefulness, she was sure that there were some moments when she lapsed into unconsciousness. But the morning brought with it the promise of another day of pain, of unprofitable preoccupation. Sometimes after she had read her Psalm, despite the fact that she had been tossing, tossing on her pillow, she yielded to an overwhelming sense of apathy and lay there motionless for hours in the security of her bed.

===Chinese===
她恨清晨甚于恨黑夜。尽管整夜清醒，她确信总有那么一些片刻，自己是不省人事的。可清晨带来的，是又一天苦痛的许诺，又一天无益萦念的许诺。有时读完了《诗篇》，尽管分明还在枕上翻来覆去、翻来覆去，她仍旧向一股排山倒海般的倦怠降服了，在床铺那份安稳无虞里，一动不动躺上几个钟头。

===Original===
Her mental agony was so great at times that it seemed almost physical.

Her condition surprised Sylvia greatly. “I never had any idea that Jan cared so much for Peter,” she told Brian. She had had to share her sister’s secret with him. Joanna’s persistent sleeplessness had led Sylvia in her protecting eagerness to pretend to Harry Portor that she herself was in need of a sedative and Harry had spoken to Brian about it. There had to be explanations.

===Chinese===
她精神上的苦痛有时剧烈到近乎肉体的地步。

她这情形叫西尔维娅大为吃惊。「我从没想到小乔对彼得这样上心，」她对布莱恩说。妹妹的秘密，她不得不与他分担。乔安娜那缠绵不去的失眠，出于保护妹妹的急切，让西尔维娅向哈里·波托尔假称自己需要一点安眠药，而哈里又把这事同布莱恩说了。总得有个交代。

===Original===
Brian was not at all surprised at Joanna’s suffering. “A girl like Joanna would be bound to feel deeply or not at all. I knew she must have really cared for Peter, else she’d have chucked him long ago. Joanna did nag at him, but Peter is really the one to blame, for standing for it. If he’d given her a piece of his mind now and then she’d have understood whom she had to deal with; Joanna thought she could treat him as she pleased. Then when he got tired of it he threw up the whole thing without any warning, the silly ass.”

===Chinese===
乔安娜的痛苦丝毫没让布莱恩意外。「像乔安娜这样的女孩，要么爱得深，要么全然不爱。我早知道她对彼得必定是动了真心，不然她早就把他甩了。乔安娜确实数落过他，可真正该怪的是彼得，怪他一味忍着。他要是时不时回敬她几句，她就会明白自己对付的是个什么样的人；乔安娜以为她想怎么摆布他就怎么摆布他。后来他烦了，一声招呼也不打，就把整件事撂了挑子，这头蠢驴。」

===Original===
“Better not let Joanna hear you call him that,” Sylvia interrupted.

He went on unnoticing. “Of course, what Joanna doesn’t realize is that she’s up against the complex of color in Peter’s life. It comes to every colored man and every colored woman, too, who has any ambition. Jan will feel it herself one day. Peter’s got it worse than most of us because he’s got such a terrible ‘mad’ on white people to start with. But every colored man feels it sooner or later. It gets in the way of his dreams, of his education, of his marriage, of the rearing of his children. The time comes when he thinks, ‘I might just as well fall back; there’s no use pushing on. A colored man just can’t make any headway in this awful country.’ Of course, it’s a fallacy. And if a fellow sticks it out he finally gets past it, but not before it has worked considerable confusion in his life. To have the ordinary job of living is bad enough, but to add to it all the thousand and one difficulties which follow simply in the train of being colored﻿—well, all I’ve got to say, Sylvia, is we’re some wonderful people to live through it all and keep our sanity.”

===Chinese===
「最好别让乔安娜听见你这样骂他，」西尔维娅插嘴道。

他浑然不觉，只管往下说：「当然，乔安娜没有意识到的是，她撞上的是彼得生命里的肤色情结（complex of color）。凡是胸怀半点抱负的有色男人——每一个有色女人也一样——都躲不开它。小乔自己早晚也会尝到。彼得比我们多数人都背得沉重，因为他一开头就对白人憋着一腔可怕的怨气。可每个有色男人迟早都会觉出它来。它横在他梦想的路上、求学的路上、婚姻的路上、养育儿女的路上。到时候他就会想：『不如干脆退回来算了；往前闯也没用。在这糟糕透顶的国家，有色男人就是出不了头。』当然，这是种谬见。一个人要是咬牙挺到底，终究能闯过它去，可在闯过去之前，它已经给他的生活搅起了不小的纷乱。光是活着这份寻常的差事就够受了，还要再加上仅仅因为身为有色人种就接踵而来的一千零一种难处——唉，西尔维娅，我只想说，我们这号人能把这一切都活过来、还保得住清醒的神志，真真是些了不得的人。」

===Original===
Sylvia agreed soberly that he was right.

“Now, Peter,” said Brian, warming to his subject, “had a lot of natural handicaps, he was poor, he had no sense of responsibility, he was never too fond of work unless he had someone to spur him on to it. In addition to that he falls in love with a girl who has everything in the world which he lacks, especially comparative ease and overwhelming ambition. Jan doesn’t see Peter and herself as two ordinary human beings, she thinks they have a high destiny to perform and so she drives Peter into a course of action which left to himself he would never pursue. I’ll bet a month’s salary Peter had no intention of studying surgery until he found out he had to do something extraordinary to win Joanna. Now, just when each needs the most sympathy from the other, when Joanna’s plans are, I suspect, going awry, and when Peter is suffering most from his color complex, the two let their frazzled nerves carry them into a jangle and bang, Peter flies to the first woman who promises to let him take life easy! Maggie doesn’t see life in the large, she’s too much taken up with getting what she wants out of her own life. Perhaps she’s right.”

===Chinese===
西尔维娅神色肃然，赞同他说得对。

「再说彼得，」布莱恩谈兴正浓，「天生就有一大堆短处：他穷，没有责任感，向来不大爱干活，除非有人鞭策着他干。偏又赶巧，他爱上了一个姑娘，世上他缺的东西她一应俱全，尤其是相对的安逸和压倒一切的雄心。小乔不把彼得和自己看成两个寻常人，她认定他们负有崇高的使命要完成，于是把彼得驱上一条他独个儿绝不会走的路。我敢拿一个月的薪水打赌，彼得本来压根无意学外科，直到他发现必须干出点超乎寻常的事，才能赢得乔安娜。而眼下，恰恰在两人最需要彼此体恤的时候——我料想乔安娜的盘算正在落空，彼得又正被他的肤色情结折磨得最苦——两人却任凭磨得稀烂的神经把自己拖进一场口角，砰的一声，彼得就飞进头一个许他轻轻松松过日子的女人怀里去了！玛吉看人生不朝大处看，她太忙着从自己的生活里捞取她想要的东西了。也许她是对的。」

===Original===
“I don’t see how you can say that, Brian.”

===Chinese===
「我不明白你怎么说得出这种话，布莱恩。」

===Original===
“Well, it all depends on one’s viewpoint. Personally, I think Peter will get what he deserves if he marries Maggie. She’s the one that astonishes me. Of course, if Peter and Jan really are through with each other, he’s got a perfect right to marry whom he pleases, but I should think Maggie’s old friendship for you two girls would have held her back awhile.” A memory stirred vaguely within him. “Or﻿—no, that would really be too rotten.”

===Chinese===
「嗯，这全看各人的眼光。依我个人想，彼得要是娶了玛吉，那是罪有应得。叫我惊奇的倒是玛吉。当然，彼得和小乔要是真的彼此恩断义绝了，他完全有权娶他中意的人，可我以为，玛吉同你们姊妹俩多年的交情，总该让她迟疑一阵子才是。」一段记忆在他心里隐隐一动。「要不——不，那样就实在太过下作了。」

===Original===
“What would?”

“Maggie, you know. Remember how suddenly she married Neal? I’ve always thought Joanna had something to do with that. Just the Sunday before, Maggie had given me a look-in on her feelings for Philip and I happened to tell Jan about it. My, how she raved! A few days later Maggie married her gambler.”

===Chinese===
「什么太过下作？」

「玛吉的事呗，你是知道的。还记得她嫁尼尔嫁得多么突然吗？我一向疑心乔安娜同那事脱不了干系。就在那前一个星期天，玛吉刚让我窥见她对菲利普的心思，我碰巧又把这告诉了小乔。好家伙，她闹得多凶！没过几天，玛吉就嫁给她那个赌徒了。」

===Original===
This was all news to Sylvia.

“Well, I won’t tell Joanna. She’s got enough to bear.”

===Chinese===
这些话对西尔维娅说来全是新闻。

「反正，我是不告诉乔安娜的。她要承受的已经够多了。」

===Original===
Joanna was indeed bearing more than Sylvia could guess. She was feeling the pull of awakened and unsatisfied passion. It is doubtful if she could thus have analyzed it, for she had rather deliberately withheld her attention from the basic facts of life. “Plenty of time for that,” she had told herself gayly, a little proud perhaps of a virginal fastidiousness which kept her ignorant as well as innocent. Yet bit by bit she had built up the idea of a shrine into which, not unwillingly, she should enter with Peter some day. She had never even vaguely thought of anyone else as a companion. Her whole concept of love and marriage for herself centered about Peter Bye.

===Chinese===
乔安娜承受着的，确实比西尔维娅所能猜度的更多。她正感到一股被唤醒而不得满足的激情在牵拽着她。她能否就这样把它剖析明白，大可存疑，因为她一向存心不肯把心思放到人生的基本事实上去。「那个，来日方长，」她曾欢快地对自己说，或许还有几分得意于一份处子的矜持——那份矜持使她既纯真又无知。然而一点一点地，她心里已筑起一座圣殿的构想，有朝一日，她将并非不情愿地同彼得一同走进那殿里去。她连模模糊糊把旁的什么人想作伴侣的念头都不曾有过。她对自身爱情与婚姻的全部构想，都系在彼得·拜一个人身上。

===Original===
And now Peter was gone﻿—and his departure had opened up this sea, this bottomless pit of torment. This, this was life. “This is being grown up,” she told herself through endless midnight watches.

===Chinese===
如今彼得走了——他的离去掀开了这片海，这口无底的苦痛深渊。这，这就是人生。「这就是长大成人，」在一夜复一夜无尽的午夜守望里，她这样对自己说。

## XXII / 二十二

===Original===
Ten months later Tom Mason leaned back against the red plush of the car seat and jingled some coins in his pocket.

“Tell you what, Bye, we really are cleaning up. I hadn’t expected anything like this run of engagements. Now suppose you beat it along to Mrs. Lea’s and find out what special arrangements she wants made for the musicians tonight and I’ll go on to Mrs. Lawlor and see about tomorrow.”

Peter stared moodily at the flying landscape. “I wish you’d come yourself, Mason. I hate to talk to these white people. Their damned patronizing airs make me sick.”

“What do you care about their patronizin’? All I’m interested in is gettin’ what I can out of them. When I’ve made my pile, if I can’t spend it here the way I please, Annie and me can pick up and go to South America or France. I hear they treat colored people all right there.”

“ ‘Treat colored people all right,’ ” Peter mimicked. “What business has anyone ‘treating’ us, anyway? The world’s ours as much as it is theirs. And I don’t want to leave America. It’s mine, my people helped make it. These very orchards we’re passing now used to be the famous Bye orchards. My grandfather and great-grandfather helped to cultivate them.”

===Chinese===
十个月后，汤姆·梅森往汽车座位的红丝绒椅背上一靠，把口袋里的硬币拨弄得叮当作响。

「跟你说，拜，咱们这回可真是赚大了。我压根儿没想到会像这样一连接下这么多演出活儿。这么着，麻烦你这就赶到利太太（Mrs. Lea）家去，问问她今晚乐师那边还有什么特别的安排要交代；我呢，上劳勒太太（Mrs. Lawlor）那儿去，把明天的事定下来。」

彼得闷闷地望着窗外飞掠而过的景色。「我倒希望你亲自去一趟，梅森。我讨厌跟这些白人打交道。他们那副该死的屈尊俯就的嘴脸，叫我恶心。」

「管他们屈尊不屈尊？我只关心能从他们身上捞多少是多少。等我攒够了本钱，要是不能在这儿随心所欲地花，我和安妮抬腿就走，去南美，去法国。听说那儿的人待有色人种还不错。」

「『待有色人种还不错』，」彼得学着腔调讥嘲道，「话说回来，这世道凭什么轮得到什么人来『待』我们？世界是他们的，也是我们的。我不想离开美国。它是我的，我的人民出过力建设它。咱们此刻路过的这些果园，从前就是赫赫有名的拜家果园。我祖父和曾祖父都曾出力垦殖。」


===Original===
“Is that so? Honest?” Tom showed a sudden respectful interest. “How’d they come to lose them?”

“Lose them? They never owned them. The black Byes were slaves of the white Byes.”

“Oh, slaves! Oh, you mean they worked in the fields? Well, I guess that’s different. Come on, here we are.”

Peter flung himself out of the car after Tom and followed him up a tree-lined street. The suburban town stretched calm, peaceful and superior about them. Clearly this was the home of the rich and wellborn. It is true that a few ordinary mortals lived here, but mainly to do the bidding of the wealthy. A group of young white girls, passing the two men, glanced at them a little curiously.

“Entertainers for the Lea affair,” one of them said, making no effort to keep from being overheard.

===Chinese===
「是吗？真的？」汤姆忽然生出几分敬意。「那后来怎么丢掉的呢？」

「丢掉？他们从来就没有拥有过。黑拜家从前是白拜家的奴隶。」

「哦，奴隶！哦，你是说他们在地里干活来着？那——那可就不一样了。走吧，到了。」

彼得跟着汤姆跳下车，随他走上一条绿树成荫的街道。这座郊外小镇在四周铺展开来，宁静、安详，透着一股高人一等的气派。这显然是富贵人家与名门望族的安家之地。固然也有几个寻常凡人住在这里，但多半是听候富人之命使唤的。一群年轻的白人姑娘从这两个男人身边走过，略带好奇地瞥了他们一眼。

「利家宴会请来的卖艺人。」其中一个说，丝毫不怕让人听见。


===Original===
Peter stopped short. “That’s what I hate,” he said fiercely. “Labeled because we’re black.”

“Ain’t you got a grouch, though!” Tom spoke almost admiringly. He told his sister afterwards: “Bye’s got this here﻿—now﻿—temper’ment. Never can tell how it’s goin’ to take him. Seems different since he started keeping company with Maggie, don’t you think so?”

Annie admitted she did.

At present Tom patted Peter on the shoulder, and starting him up the driveway which led to Mrs. Lea’s large low white house, went on himself to Mrs. Lawlor.

===Chinese===
彼得猛地收住脚步。「我最恨的就是这个，」他狠狠地说，「就因为我们是黑人，就被打上标签。」

「瞧你这脾气可真不小！」汤姆说这话时几乎带着佩服。他事后对妹妹说：「拜这家伙呀，天生这么一股子——喏——『脾气』。啥时候犯起来可没个准。自打他跟玛吉处上以后，人好像变了些，你说是不是？」

安妮承认是变了些。

这会儿汤姆拍了拍彼得的肩膀，把他打发上那条通往利太太家宽大低矮的白色宅子的汽车道，自己径直往劳勒太太家去了。


===Original===
Mrs. Lea received Peter in a small morning-room. She was pretty, a genuine blonde, with small delicate features and beautiful fluffy hair. But as Peter did not like fair types, his mind simply registered “washed-out,” and took no further stock of her looks. What he did notice was that she was dressed in a lacey, too transparent floating robe, too low in the neck, and too short in the skirt.

“Something she would wear only before someone for whom she cared very much, or someone whom she didn’t think worth considering,” he told himself, lowering.

Mrs. Lea, leading him into the ballroom beyond, barely glanced at him. “See, the musicians are to sit behind those palms and the piano will be completely banked with flowers. I’m expecting the decorators every moment. Your men will have to get here very early so as to get behind all this without being seen. I want the effect of music instead of perfume pouring out of the flowers. Do you get the idea﻿—er﻿—what did you say your name was?”

“Yes, I understand,” said Peter shortly. “My name is Bye.”

===Chinese===
利太太在一间小小的晨间起居室里接待了彼得。她生得漂亮，是位真正的金发美人，五官纤巧，一头蓬松的秀发。可彼得不喜欢白皙的类型，心里只落下一句「素淡无光」的评语，对她的容貌便不再多加打量。他倒是注意到，她身穿一件蕾丝重重、过分透薄的飘飘长袍，领口开得太低，裙摆又嫌太短。

「这样的衣裳，她只会在两种人面前穿：不是她十分在意的人，就是她认为不值一顾的人。」他暗自思忖，脸色沉了下来。

利太太领他走进里面的舞厅，几乎没朝他看一眼。「瞧，乐师们要坐在那些棕榈后面，钢琴将整个用鲜花围起来。布置会场的人随时会到。你们的人得早早赶来，好赶在这一切布置妥当之前躲到后头去，不让人瞧见。我要的是音乐仿佛从花丛里倾泻出来的效果，而不是花香。你明白我的意思吧——呃——你刚才说你贵姓来着？」

「是，我明白。」彼得简慢地答道。「我姓拜。」


===Original===
“I meant your first name﻿—Bye﻿—why, that’s the name of a family in Bryn Mawr, who used to own half of the land about here. There’re a Dr. Meriwether Bye and his grandfather, Dr. Meriwether Bye, living in the old Bye house now. Where do you come from?”

“I was born in Philadelphia like my father and grandfather and his father before him.”

She stated the obvious conclusion: “Probably your parents belonged to the Bryn Mawr Byes.”

“So my father told me,” replied Peter, affecting a composure equal to her own. “His name was Meriwether Bye.”

===Chinese===
「我问的是你的名字——拜——哟，布林莫尔有一户人家正姓这个姓，从前这一带一半的土地都是他们的。如今老拜家的宅子里住着一位梅里韦瑟·拜医生，还有他的祖父——也是一位梅里韦瑟·拜医生。你是哪里人？」

「我生在费城，我父亲、我祖父，还有他的父亲，一辈辈都是。」

她顺理成章地道出了结论：「那你父母多半原本属于布林莫尔的拜家。」

「我父亲正是这么跟我说的，」彼得答道，装出一副不输于她的镇定。「他就叫梅里韦瑟·拜。」


===Original===
She did not like that. She decided she did not like him either﻿—eyeing his straight, fine figure and meeting his unyielding look. These niggers with their uppish ways! Besides this one looked, looked﻿—indefinably he reminded her of young Meriwether Bye. She spoke to him:

“I don’t want you to leave tonight before I get a chance to point you out to young Dr. Bye. He’ll be so interested.” She looked at Peter again. Yes, he was intelligent enough to get the full force of what she wanted to say. “It’s so in keeping with things that the grandson of the man who was slave to his grandfather should be his entertainer tonight.”

Peter felt his skin tightening. “I’m afraid you’ll be disappointed. I’m a medical student, not an entertainer. I came here for Mr. Mason, who is very busy. You may be sure I’ll give him your instructions. Good day, Mrs. Lea.”

He rushed out of the house, down to the station where, without waiting for Tom, he boarded the train. Not far from the West Philadelphia depot he pushed the bell of a certain house, flung open the unlocked door and rushed up a flight of stairs.

===Chinese===
这话她不爱听。她断定自己也不喜欢这个人——一面打量他挺拔出色的身量，一面迎上他毫不退让的目光。这些黑鬼，一个个傲气冲天！何况这一个长得，长得——说不清怎么，就是让她想起年轻的梅里韦瑟·拜。她开口对他说：

「今晚你可别急着走，我还要把你指给小拜医生瞧瞧呢。他准会大有兴趣。」她又看了彼得一眼。不错，他够聪明，足能掂出她这话里十足的分量。「这多合乎世道啊——当年给人家祖父为奴的那人的孙子，今晚来给人家当卖艺人。」

彼得觉得自己的皮肤绷紧了。「恐怕您要失望了。我是医学院学生，不是卖艺人。我是替梅森先生来的，他实在忙得很。您放心，您的吩咐我一定转达给他。再会，利太太。」

他冲出宅子，直奔车站，没等汤姆便上了火车。离西费城车站不远，他按响一栋房子的门铃，推开没上锁的门，蹬蹬蹬奔上一段楼梯。


===Original===
In a small room to his left he found the person he was seeking, a short, almost black young fellow who lifted a dejected and then an amazed countenance toward him.

“Am I seeing things? Where’d you blow in from, Pete? Thought you’d chucked us all, the old school and all the rest of it.”

“I haven’t, I’ve been a fool, a damned fool, but I’m back to my senses. I’m going back to my classes and I tell you, Ed Morgan, I’ll clean up. See here, you’ve got to do me a favor.”

“Name it.”

===Chinese===
在左手一间小屋里，他找到了要找的人——一个矮个子、肤色深得近乎纯黑的年轻人，朝他抬起一张先是沮丧、继而惊愕的脸。

「我不是眼花了吧？你打哪儿冒出来的，彼得？还当你把我们全都甩了，把老学校什么的全都甩了呢。」

「我没有，我先前是个傻瓜，十足的傻瓜，如今清醒过来了。我要回去上课了，我告诉你，埃德·摩根（Ed Morgan），我会干出名堂来的。听着，你得帮我个忙。」

「你说吧。」


===Original===
“You know Mason, Tom Mason on Fifteenth Street? I’ve been playing for him. But I can’t stick it any longer. Tom’s all right, but I can’t stand his customers. Besides, I’ve got to get back to work. I’m quitting this minute﻿—see. But Tom’s got a big dance on, near Bryn Mawr tonight at a Mrs.﻿—Mrs. Lea,” he gulped. “Good pay and all that. You can play as well as I can, Ed. Easy stuff, you can read it. You got to do it.”

“Do it! Man, lead me to that job. I’m broke, see, stony broke, busted.” He turned his pockets inside out. “I was just wondering what I could pawn. And I need instruments﻿—Oh, Lord!”

Peter gave him some money. “Take this, you can pay me any time. Only rush down to Tom’s and tell him I can’t come. I’m dead﻿—see?﻿—drowned, fallen in the Schuylkill. And see here, old fellow, afterwards we’ll have a talk. I want everything, everything, mind you, that you can remember, every note, every bit of paper that bears on the work of these last ten months. And I’ll show them﻿—” he seemed to forget Morgan﻿—“with their damned talk of entertainers.” Down the stairs he ran, still talking.

“Mad, quite mad,” said little Morgan, staring. “Glad he’s coming back to work, though. Now, where’d I put that cap?”

===Chinese===
